| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.


# Background (not graded): `launch-global-state-question.md`

# Shared global state at native launch boundaries

Round-2 finding `bp-acm` requires a contract decision about the native caller
adapter. This document requests that decision; it does not adopt a new protocol
or claim that the bug is fixed.

Dialect 0.2.10 §4.3 requires one classical memory shared by H, CC, R and L
entry state. Section 19.4 preserves native H callers unless pointer-rich sharing
requires promotion. The mixed-object requirement `bennett-4ht` also preserves
ordinary native callers and multiple selected module entries.

The ISA 0.8 §10 C API supplies program/run ownership, typed arguments/results,
host requests, reversal and close/unload. Its published header has no operation
to bind, import or export a classical memory range. A layout image supplies
initial data, not shared native storage. Using a native pointer as an ISA
integer address does not connect the two memories.

The regression `tests/review_regressions.py --major M --case global-counter`
links the emitted host bitcode with the published VM library and tests three
observations on both LLVM 18 and 23:

| Native caller actions | Required output | Current output |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Call `top` twice | `1 2 host_global=2` | `1 1 host_global=0` |
| Call `top`, set native `g=40`, call again | `1 41 host_global=41` | `1 1 host_global=40` |
| Call `top`, then another marked entry sharing `g` | `1 2 host_global=2` | `1 1 host_global=0` |

The first row alone could tempt a program cache. A cache still leaves native
`g` unchanged and cannot import the intervening H write. Separate caches for
entries also retain separate device copies of the same source global. Neither
is a fix for §4.3. Promoting every native caller would change §19.4 placement.

The requested decision is the supported classical-state bridge for this
boundary: may the producer publish a versioned copy-in/writeback service using
existing ISA host requests, or should the launch contract supply explicit
memory-transfer/binding operations? Existing integer arguments/results could
also carry bytes through a generated adapter, but that would still need a
published mapping and ownership convention, not an unchecked host-pointer cast.

Whichever route is selected, its publication must specify:

- Source global identity, selected component ownership and native/device
  address correspondence, including globals shared by several entries.
- Copy-in of current H bytes and copy-out of device writes, including aliases,
  with the §19.4 promotion rule for pointer-rich reachable storage.
- Program context lifetime, compatible component linking, and handle/token
  namespaces; a function-local static cache cannot decide these for the native
  container.
- Memory ownership while a run is active, writeback of completed or faulted
  prefixes, and how reversal restores the host-visible state before subsequent
  H mutation or reuse.
- The adapter service/schema or C API, and the component metadata that lets
  plugin, driver, runtime and executor independently implement the same bridge.

This requests the smallest bridge needed to implement the existing shared
memory semantics. It does not request whole-process-only placement, private
access to VM memory, a change to the ISA 0.8 inner object grammar, or a silent
ISA upgrade. The general mixed-object audit is deferred; the determining
blocker here is the concrete P0 loss of an ordinary shared scalar global.


# The document under review (round 1 snapshot)

# F04/F08/F23 — native calls and registered memory

Beads: **b-isa-spec-x1y**, **b-isa-spec-6ma**, **bennett-vm-8v0** (contract side only). This is an amendment proposal, never a prerequisite for rungs 3/4. F30 remains deferred until rung 3 or 4 lands. No launch ABI or backend is implemented here.

## Exact defect and clause citations

[ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md) §10 binds INT/F64/QREG/TOKEN to device CC parameters and copies argument/entropy buffers. It has neither an ordinary `ptr` binding nor an aggregate/native calling convention. §§7.1–7.2 initialise device-owned memory and allocation metadata from `layout`; an integer holding native pointer bits supplies neither liveness nor access authority. §0.3's deferred ISA-11 cannot substitute for a published native-memory contract.

[AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F04/F08/F23 correctly identifies a mixed-case lock-out. The existing by-value launch works within its stated domain; this is not a reproduced failure of a host-sharing backend. F23's actual bead now cites [VM PLAN, proposal 0.1](../../../../bennett-vm/docs/PLAN.md), which already sketches registrations, leases and eleven future vectors. That design is useful input, not a published cross-repository interface or executed integration evidence. No duplicate F23 implementation note is needed.

## North Star gap

This is a real missing boundary capability for North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4: native main must call marked modules while preserving ordinary pointer-rich sharing. Promoting main or copying unrelated buffers cannot be the only eventual realization. Existing rungs and device lowering remain valid and independent of this amendment.

## Alternatives and witnesses

| Construction | Witness and consequence |
|---|---|
| Treat `ptr` as INT | Native address 0x1000 can denote unmapped device storage, a different global, or stale storage after reuse. Width agreement supplies no ownership. Unsound as registration. |
| Snapshot into independent buffers | Two native pointers to one interior cell must observe the same write; two independent copies can disagree. Useful only with an explicit alias-preserving import/export protocol. |
| Promote all pointer-sharing callers | Expresses device computation but converts native main in the cyclic graph case. Preserve as an option; it does not witness the mixed capability. |
| Versioned registration and native adapter | Canonical allocation identities, alias-preserving address observations and leases support native calls and reversal. Recommended. Same-address and imported-private-oracle profiles have distinct evidence. |

Concrete acceptance witness: native bytes `[10,20,30,40]`; call A adds 3 to byte 1 and closes, yielding `[10,23,30,40]`. Native code sets byte 0 to 99. Call B XORs byte 0 with 1; reversing B **before close** restores `[99,23,30,40]`. A stale first-call snapshot would incorrectly restore 10. A second witness has two cyclic nodes and two arguments to one interior value: write 7 through one alias, read 7 through the other, preserve pointer equality and address-derived outputs. The finite model in [verify.py](verify.py) checks the bookkeeping and alias counterexample; it is not VM execution.

## Recommended exact spec diff

Append to §10 Model, and cross-reference from §§7.1–7.2:

```text
Native process entry and device CC entry are separate roles. A mixed
container MAY expose a native callable symbol whose adapter selects a
device program and CC entry. launch 0.9's existing argument kinds remain
by-value; integer bits do not register native storage. Native calls with
ordinary pointers or aggregates use an explicitly negotiated mixed-launch
and memory profile. The following requirements govern that profile.

The adapter descriptor binds container component/local-entry, linked ISA
object_hash, entry signature and closure_hash, target ABI/triple/DataLayout,
layout/mapping profile, certificate/build record, and runtime/service
versions. It specifies positional scalar and aggregate decomposition,
field offsets, padding treatment (unobservable padding is zero-filled),
endianness, integer widths and exact float bits. Data pointers, function
pointers, opaque launch ids and tokens have distinct descriptor kinds.
Pointers are capabilities plus checked offsets; raw integers grant no
native access. Null and one-past values retain their source semantics;
one-past is not a dereferenceable byte.

A registration records canonical allocation identity and generation,
owner/liveness authority, extent, permissions, target layout and mapping,
allocator metadata policy, and current entry epoch. Interior/overlapping
views resolve to that same identity. Mapping preserves aliasing and all
defined pointer observations, including comparisons, ptrtoint/address
hashing and indirect dispatch; it MUST NOT silently swizzle unidentified
integer words. A same-address profile requires matching representation and
nonconflicting ranges. Other mappings require a separately published
observation-preserving encoding/relocation protocol, not a capability cap.

Before access, validate nonwrapping ranges, generation, liveness, layout,
permissions and ownership. Device-private allocations keep §7.1 semantics.
Imported storage supplies equivalent bounds/liveness/allocation metadata;
it does not require native malloc to have executed the device bump history.
Escaping allocations require a host-compatible allocation/transfer profile
covering native free/realloc, aliases, budgets and reversible metadata.
A private device address alone cannot become a returned native pointer.

For the exclusive mutable profile, acquire a coordinated lease before the
run and retain it through CLOSED. All aliases and programs sharing that
storage participate in the same coordinator. Host/free/realloc/unregister
and conflicting runs cannot access it during the lease; compatible read
leases and disjoint storage may coexist. The liveness authority must
prevent or detect invalidation; hashing entry bytes is not an interference
protocol for arbitrary unsynchronised native writes.

Each invocation binds program/root/run, arguments, registration generations
and entry epochs. COMPLETED/FAILED retain ownership. Eligible reverse
restores this invocation's entry bytes and allocator/ownership history
before close, retaining §10's measurement/free restrictions. A failed
operation makes no partial change; earlier successful operations remain
in the failed prefix and reverse under §§7.4/10. Close commits the exact
remaining prefix and releases leases. This is synchronised visibility,
not atomic publication of an arbitrary graph. After close a later native
edit is part of the next invocation's entry state.

The synchronous adapter waits through HOST_REQUEST and suspension under
§10, distinguishes API failure from a FAILED device result/fault, extracts
declared results, closes and returns to the native caller. An asynchronous
adapter returns an opaque run owner; it does not grant early access to
leased memory. No exception crosses the C boundary implicitly. Profile
negotiation, stale/wrong-owner/unregistered pointer, range/layout mismatch,
interference and failed-operation diagnostics are distinct published
status outcomes; assign their wire encodings with the new ABI version.
Do not allocate numeric tags in launch 0.9's existing enum.

Lifted/oracle memory is a private coherent construction. Import a
consistent entry image and equivalent metadata before private allocation,
then retain the prescribed forward/reverse semantics. A quantum address
does not cause a native RAM read. Current copied launches and snapshot/
replay remain admitted constructions, not the sole eventual native-sharing
capability. Device effects, CC replies and native I/O retain separate owners.
```

Publish the descriptor and C wire layouts as one versioned companion contract before implementing it in driver/runtime/VM. The text deliberately leaves numeric encodings for that publication, rather than presenting a partial C header as ready to consume. F23 should consume this publication and the F15/F16 mapping rules; retain the VM's existing integer-address backend and by-value ABI meanwhile.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Runtime registrations/addresses belong to invocation records, never immutable ISA source identities. Container bytes/digest, payload bytes/digest, `object_hash`, root closure, certificate and runtime profile are separate bindings. Changed imported images/layouts or generated wrappers can change actual object/closure/schedule/certificate hashes. A memory profile alone does not change `ref@0.9` recipe bytes. Driver, runtimes, VM, plugin and conformance must pin the accepted contract independently; no consumer imports VM source as the protocol.

## Conformance vectors and actual verification

Require scalar/F64/QREG/TOKEN compatibility; aggregate padding and float-bit round trips; both concrete witnesses above; interior/overlapping/cyclic aliases; host edits between closed runs; free/reuse at the same address; null/one-past/range overflow; stale/wrong-program generations; conflicting leases; failed-store prefix and reverse; measurement ineligibility; returned host-owned allocations and native realloc; function pointers; two embedded roots; lifted private import and metadata-only invalidation. Compare the same materialised device closure and layout, preserving original pointer identity for address-sensitive baselines. Unregistered numeric CLI pointers and `--mem` hex copies are negative evidence controls. These are proposed integration vectors, not claims of an implemented backend. [INDEX](INDEX.md) records the actual bounded checks.

## K entry: 0.9 versus 0.10

Recommend a versioned companion `mixed-launch/memory` contract, normatively referenced from ISA 0.10 and the next dialect revision. A 0.9 K entry may clarify the existing by-value boundary, but cannot add pointer tags or memory semantics silently. Any in-version K route needs **council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules**, including negotiation and status encodings. Assign numeric K ids only on acceptance. No recipe catalog change is required by this design alone.

## Mixed fat object

Apply [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F04/F08/F23. Native main, argv/envp and host-only libc remain native; only marked modules and their complete device closures enter ISA inventory. Each root keeps its own certificate/context `[]`, budget/ledger and run/tape association. Shared storage uses explicit coordination across those roots. This keeps the mixed fat object reachable; it neither implements it nor makes its amendments prerequisites for rungs 3/4. F30 stays deferred.

codex
I’m using the repository’s Beads guidance and checking the proposed rules against ISA 0.9, the cited audit findings, and the consumer’s three shared-global observations.

name: beads

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# Contract derivations — review index and reproducible verification

These are proposals for council grading, not published spec changes.
Contract beads remain open for acceptance/implementation; b5b's owner
handles its implementation status. No git command was run by this job.

A recommendation for a 0.9 K repair means the existing council-reviewed
repair track, not permission to publish an incompatible change silently.
`spec/README.md` normally requires a full-version bump for semantic
changes. Council must settle that publication choice. Every statement
below that existing source hashes stay fixed assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged envelope bytes; a full-version bump
instead changes all symbol/interface/closure envelopes and their consumer
pins. Catalog bytes/hash change only when that separately versioned
catalog is actually changed. No note here performs either publication.

| Item | Disposition | Proposed release |
|---|---|---|
| [z4c](2026-11-02-contract-z4c.md) | Missing action support addresses; blocks existing legal source | 0.9 K repair |
| [l2p](2026-11-02-contract-l2p.md) | Clean-isometry mechanism plus explicit North Star measurement-cleanup gap | 0.9 semantic K repair, versioned expansion |
| [fd0](2026-11-02-contract-fd0.md) | Missing symbolic/import hierarchy contract; scalar-instance path is incomplete scope | 0.10 formats, current explicit blocker |
| [lpe](2026-11-02-contract-lpe.md) | Compact width-family extension; existing dispatch has a practical emission limit | 0.10 |

## Exact arithmetic and document check

Run this extraction from the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
python3 -I -B - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-verification.md')
source = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
exec(compile(source, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

The arithmetic is in Q[z]/(z^4+1), z=exp(i*pi/4), with rational
coefficients; H uses (z−z³)/2. There are no floating-point quantum
calculations in this check. Equality of every branch operator to V/sqrt(2)
establishes the resulting channel on all matrix units, hence also with
an external reference; the negative vectors compare density matrices.
This is exact finite mathematical verification, not a producer certificate
accepted by an implementation of the proposed contract.

```python
from fractions import Fraction as F
from pathlib import Path
from math import gcd
import hashlib
import re

def scalar(n):
    return (F(n), F(0), F(0), F(0))

ZERO, ONE = scalar(0), scalar(1)
Z = (F(0), F(1), F(0), F(0))

def add(a, b):
    return tuple(x + y for x, y in zip(a, b))

def neg(a):
    return tuple(-x for x in a)

def mul(a, b):
    out = [F(0)] * 7
    for i in range(4):
        for j in range(4):
            out[i+j] += a[i] * b[j]
    for k in range(6, 3, -1):
        out[k-4] -= out[k]
    return tuple(out[:4])

def conj(a):
    out = [a[0], F(0), F(0), F(0)]
    for k in range(1, 4):
        out[4-k] -= a[k]
    return tuple(out)

I = mul(Z, Z)
TINV = conj(Z)
HFACTOR = (F(0), F(1, 2), F(0), F(-1, 2))
assert mul(HFACTOR, HFACTOR) == scalar(F(1, 2))
assert mul(Z, TINV) == ONE
assert mul(I, I) == neg(ONE)

def compact(state):
    return {i: a for i, a in state.items() if a != ZERO}

def accumulate(state, i, a):
    state[i] = add(state.get(i, ZERO), a)

def enabled(i, controls):
    return all((i >> c) & 1 for c in controls)

def hadamard(state, wire, controls=()):
    out = {}
    mask = 1 << wire
    for i, a in state.items():
        if not enabled(i, controls):
            accumulate(out, i, a)
            continue
        a = mul(a, HFACTOR)
        low = i & ~mask
        accumulate(out, low, a)
        accumulate(out, low | mask, neg(a) if i & mask else a)
    return compact(out)

def flip(state, target, controls=()):
    out = {}
    for i, a in state.items():
        j = i ^ (1 << target) if enabled(i, controls) else i
        accumulate(out, j, a)
    return compact(out)

def phase(state, target, factor, controls=()):
    return compact({i: mul(a, factor) if (i >> target) & 1
                    and enabled(i, controls) else a
                    for i, a in state.items()})

# Bit positions: a=0, b=1, g=2, y=3, outer q=4.
C = [('h', 2), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)), ('tdg', 2),
     ('cx', (1, 2)), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)),
     ('tdg', 2), ('h', 2), ('sdg', 2)]
adj = {'h': 'h', 'cx': 'cx', 't': 'tdg', 'tdg': 't',
       's': 'sdg', 'sdg': 's'}
CDAG = [(adj[op], args) for op, args in reversed(C)]

def sequence(state, gates, controls=()):
    for op, args in gates:
        if op == 'h':
            state = hadamard(state, args, controls)
        elif op == 'cx':
            state = flip(state, args[1], (args[0],) + controls)
        else:
            factor = {'t': Z, 'tdg': TINV, 's': I, 'sdg': neg(I)}[op]
            state = phase(state, args, factor, controls)
    return state

for a in range(2):
    for b in range(2):
        i = a | (b << 1)
        assert sequence({i: ONE}, C) == {i | ((a*b) << 2): ONE}
        dirty_phase = I if (a, b) == (0, 1) else neg(I)
        assert sequence({i | 4: ONE}, C) == {
            i | ((1 ^ (a*b)) << 2): dirty_phase}

def expected(state, controlled, closed):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = flip(state, 3, (0, 1) + outer)
    return hadamard(state, 0, outer) if closed else state

def branches(state, controlled=False, closed=False, correction='correct'):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = sequence(state, C, outer)
    state = flip(state, 3, (2,) + outer)
    if closed:
        state = hadamard(state, 0, outer)
    state = hadamard(state, 2)  # Unconditional X-basis measurement.
    result = []
    for m in range(2):
        branch = {i: a for i, a in state.items() if ((i >> 2) & 1) == m}
        if m:
            branch = flip(branch, 2)  # Reset measured private wire.
            transport = closed and correction != 'stale'
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
            if correction != 'none':
                controls = (0, 1) + (() if correction == 'uncontrolled' else outer)
                branch = {i: neg(a) if enabled(i, controls) else a
                          for i, a in branch.items()}
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
        result.append(compact(branch))
    return result

branch_columns = 0
for controlled in (False, True):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    for i in range(32):
        if i & 4:
            continue
        start = {i: ONE}
        unitary = sequence(start, C, outer)
        unitary = flip(unitary, 3, (2,) + outer)
        unitary = sequence(unitary, CDAG, outer)
        assert unitary == expected(start, controlled, False)
        for closed in (False, True):
            want = {j: mul(a, HFACTOR)
                    for j, a in expected(start, controlled, closed).items()}
            for branch in branches(start, controlled, closed):
                assert branch == compact(want)
                branch_columns += 1
assert branch_columns == 128

def density(states):
    out = {}
    for state in states:
        for i, a in state.items():
            for j, b in state.items():
                accumulate(out, (i, j), mul(a, conj(b)))
    return compact(out)

superposition = {0: ONE}
for wire in (0, 1, 4):
    superposition = hadamard(superposition, wire)
for controlled, closed, correction in [
        (False, False, 'none'), (True, False, 'uncontrolled'),
        (False, True, 'stale')]:
    actual = density(branches(superposition, controlled, closed, correction))
    ideal = density([expected(superposition, controlled, closed)])
    assert actual != ideal, correction

# Imported model, unit conversion, sequence/parallel accounting.
def model(d):
    return 3*2*d, 2*d*d, F(1, 10) * F(1, 100)**((d+1)//2)
assert model(5) == (30, 50, F(1, 10**7))
assert model(3) == (18, 18, F(1, 100000))
assert F(30, 10**9) == F(3, 10**8)
assert 3*30*50 == 4500
assert (3*30, 50) == (90, 50)  # Sequential time/peak.
assert (30, 3*50) == (30, 150)  # Parallel time/peak.

# Width dispatch, persistent versus reversed load state.
assert (1 ^ 0, 5 ^ 3) == (1, 6)
def later_run(reverse):
    memory, bump = 7, 17  # A stored 7 and allocated at 16.
    if reverse:
        memory, bump = 0, 16
    return memory, bump
assert later_run(False) == (7, 17)
assert later_run(True) == (0, 16)

# Fixed-image coverage; hashing raw bytes differs from hashing hex text.
image_hash = hashlib.sha256(bytes([1, 2])).hexdigest()
assert image_hash != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
assert 2**(1+1+4) == 64
assert F(64, 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
assert (2**16)*8 == 524288

# Exact modular orbit; no enormous enumeration.
for seed in (0, 3, 4):
    visited, current = set(), seed
    while current not in visited:
        visited.add(current)
        current = (current + 8) % 256
    assert visited == {x for x in range(256) if (x-seed) % 8 == 0}
    assert len(visited) == 32
assert (2**64)//gcd(8, 2**64) == 2**61

# Controlled X and -X produce orthogonal Bell states.
bell_plus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: HFACTOR}
bell_minus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: neg(HFACTOR)}
inner = ZERO
for i, a in bell_plus.items():
    inner = add(inner, mul(conj(a), bell_minus[i]))
assert inner == ZERO

# Fault counts concern code(F_B), not the incoming XOR word.
assert 4*4*16 == 256
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0  # Code 1 failure can leave final fault word zero.
assert 1 ^ 0 == 1  # Successful invocation can leave it nonzero.
assert (1+1, 2*2, max(1, 2)) == (2, 4, 2)  # Root time/occupancy/peak.
assert 1+2 != 2  # Child peaks cannot be summed to get root peak.

# Owned deliverable structure and local links.
base = Path('docs/notes')
ids = ['z4c', 'l2p', 'fd0', 'lpe', 'adg', 'xv4', 'b5b', '5u3',
       'checker-native-model', 'checker-diagnostics', 'checker-fault-rate',
       'checker-resource-contexts']
for ident in ids:
    path = base / ('2026-11-02-contract-' + ident + '.md')
    body = path.read_text()
    for word in ['Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                 'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes',
                 'Conformance', 'Release']:
        assert word.lower() in body.lower(), (ident, word)
    assert not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', body)
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in body.splitlines()) % 2 == 0
    for target in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', body):
        if not target.startswith(('https://', 'http://', '#')):
            assert (path.parent / target.split('#')[0]).exists(), (ident, target)
print('PASS: 12 proposals; exact AND 8 columns/32 inverse columns; '
      '128 corrected branch columns; 3 negative channels; '
      'model/domain/orbit/resource arithmetic; fences and local links')
print('Fixed-image SHA-256:', image_hash)
```

## Verification limits

The exact checks establish the finite witnesses and internal document
consistency. They do not prove the proposed general contract sound in
all cases, accept a new K entry, close an implementation bead, or show
that SQLite's full reference analysis converges in practical time.
Each item states its own remaining acceptance vectors and consumer work.

 succeeded in 0ms:
# spec/

`Bennett-ISA-Spec.md` is the normative text: draft 0.8, **accepted by the
three-model council on 2026-10-28 (round 8, all members accept, no blocking
issues, mean 4.17/5)**. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett`
meta repo (`docs/council/isa-2026-10-28/`, `tools/council.py`).

Rules for changing it:

- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (section 1); drafts
  are not envelope-compatible, so the four example hashes in section 16.3
  change with it. Run `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py --repatch`
  then `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py`.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every
  time it was tried.
- Add every new example or stated result to `isa_check.py` before anyone
  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their
  defaults; an implementer who must choose something not covered there
  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.
- Revisions go back through the council.
# F04/F08/F23 — native calls and registered memory

Beads: **b-isa-spec-x1y**, **b-isa-spec-6ma**, **bennett-vm-8v0** (contract side only). This is an amendment proposal, never a prerequisite for rungs 3/4. F30 remains deferred until rung 3 or 4 lands. No launch ABI or backend is implemented here.

## Exact defect and clause citations

[ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md) §10 binds INT/F64/QREG/TOKEN to device CC parameters and copies argument/entropy buffers. It has neither an ordinary `ptr` binding nor an aggregate/native calling convention. §§7.1–7.2 initialise device-owned memory and allocation metadata from `layout`; an integer holding native pointer bits supplies neither liveness nor access authority. §0.3's deferred ISA-11 cannot substitute for a published native-memory contract.

[AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F04/F08/F23 correctly identifies a mixed-case lock-out. The existing by-value launch works within its stated domain; this is not a reproduced failure of a host-sharing backend. F23's actual bead now cites [VM PLAN, proposal 0.1](../../../../bennett-vm/docs/PLAN.md), which already sketches registrations, leases and eleven future vectors. That design is useful input, not a published cross-repository interface or executed integration evidence. No duplicate F23 implementation note is needed.

## North Star gap

This is a real missing boundary capability for North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4: native main must call marked modules while preserving ordinary pointer-rich sharing. Promoting main or copying unrelated buffers cannot be the only eventual realization. Existing rungs and device lowering remain valid and independent of this amendment.

## Alternatives and witnesses

| Construction | Witness and consequence |
|---|---|
| Treat `ptr` as INT | Native address 0x1000 can denote unmapped device storage, a different global, or stale storage after reuse. Width agreement supplies no ownership. Unsound as registration. |
| Snapshot into independent buffers | Two native pointers to one interior cell must observe the same write; two independent copies can disagree. Useful only with an explicit alias-preserving import/export protocol. |
| Promote all pointer-sharing callers | Expresses device computation but converts native main in the cyclic graph case. Preserve as an option; it does not witness the mixed capability. |
| Versioned registration and native adapter | Canonical allocation identities, alias-preserving address observations and leases support native calls and reversal. Recommended. Same-address and imported-private-oracle profiles have distinct evidence. |

Concrete acceptance witness: native bytes `[10,20,30,40]`; call A adds 3 to byte 1 and closes, yielding `[10,23,30,40]`. Native code sets byte 0 to 99. Call B XORs byte 0 with 1; reversing B **before close** restores `[99,23,30,40]`. A stale first-call snapshot would incorrectly restore 10. A second witness has two cyclic nodes and two arguments to one interior value: write 7 through one alias, read 7 through the other, preserve pointer equality and address-derived outputs. The finite model in [verify.py](verify.py) checks the bookkeeping and alias counterexample; it is not VM execution.

## Recommended exact spec diff

Append to §10 Model, and cross-reference from §§7.1–7.2:

```text
Native process entry and device CC entry are separate roles. A mixed
container MAY expose a native callable symbol whose adapter selects a
device program and CC entry. launch 0.9's existing argument kinds remain
by-value; integer bits do not register native storage. Native calls with
ordinary pointers or aggregates use an explicitly negotiated mixed-launch
and memory profile. The following requirements govern that profile.

The adapter descriptor binds container component/local-entry, linked ISA
object_hash, entry signature and closure_hash, target ABI/triple/DataLayout,
layout/mapping profile, certificate/build record, and runtime/service
versions. It specifies positional scalar and aggregate decomposition,
field offsets, padding treatment (unobservable padding is zero-filled),
endianness, integer widths and exact float bits. Data pointers, function
pointers, opaque launch ids and tokens have distinct descriptor kinds.
Pointers are capabilities plus checked offsets; raw integers grant no
native access. Null and one-past values retain their source semantics;
one-past is not a dereferenceable byte.

A registration records canonical allocation identity and generation,
owner/liveness authority, extent, permissions, target layout and mapping,
allocator metadata policy, and current entry epoch. Interior/overlapping
views resolve to that same identity. Mapping preserves aliasing and all
defined pointer observations, including comparisons, ptrtoint/address
hashing and indirect dispatch; it MUST NOT silently swizzle unidentified
integer words. A same-address profile requires matching representation and
nonconflicting ranges. Other mappings require a separately published
observation-preserving encoding/relocation protocol, not a capability cap.

Before access, validate nonwrapping ranges, generation, liveness, layout,
permissions and ownership. Device-private allocations keep §7.1 semantics.
Imported storage supplies equivalent bounds/liveness/allocation metadata;
it does not require native malloc to have executed the device bump history.
Escaping allocations require a host-compatible allocation/transfer profile
covering native free/realloc, aliases, budgets and reversible metadata.
A private device address alone cannot become a returned native pointer.

For the exclusive mutable profile, acquire a coordinated lease before the
run and retain it through CLOSED. All aliases and programs sharing that
storage participate in the same coordinator. Host/free/realloc/unregister
and conflicting runs cannot access it during the lease; compatible read
leases and disjoint storage may coexist. The liveness authority must
prevent or detect invalidation; hashing entry bytes is not an interference
protocol for arbitrary unsynchronised native writes.

Each invocation binds program/root/run, arguments, registration generations
and entry epochs. COMPLETED/FAILED retain ownership. Eligible reverse
restores this invocation's entry bytes and allocator/ownership history
before close, retaining §10's measurement/free restrictions. A failed
operation makes no partial change; earlier successful operations remain
in the failed prefix and reverse under §§7.4/10. Close commits the exact
remaining prefix and releases leases. This is synchronised visibility,
not atomic publication of an arbitrary graph. After close a later native
edit is part of the next invocation's entry state.

The synchronous adapter waits through HOST_REQUEST and suspension under
§10, distinguishes API failure from a FAILED device result/fault, extracts
declared results, closes and returns to the native caller. An asynchronous
adapter returns an opaque run owner; it does not grant early access to
leased memory. No exception crosses the C boundary implicitly. Profile
negotiation, stale/wrong-owner/unregistered pointer, range/layout mismatch,
interference and failed-operation diagnostics are distinct published
status outcomes; assign their wire encodings with the new ABI version.
Do not allocate numeric tags in launch 0.9's existing enum.

Lifted/oracle memory is a private coherent construction. Import a
consistent entry image and equivalent metadata before private allocation,
then retain the prescribed forward/reverse semantics. A quantum address
does not cause a native RAM read. Current copied launches and snapshot/
replay remain admitted constructions, not the sole eventual native-sharing
capability. Device effects, CC replies and native I/O retain separate owners.
```

Publish the descriptor and C wire layouts as one versioned companion contract before implementing it in driver/runtime/VM. The text deliberately leaves numeric encodings for that publication, rather than presenting a partial C header as ready to consume. F23 should consume this publication and the F15/F16 mapping rules; retain the VM's existing integer-address backend and by-value ABI meanwhile.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Runtime registrations/addresses belong to invocation records, never immutable ISA source identities. Container bytes/digest, payload bytes/digest, `object_hash`, root closure, certificate and runtime profile are separate bindings. Changed imported images/layouts or generated wrappers can change actual object/closure/schedule/certificate hashes. A memory profile alone does not change `ref@0.9` recipe bytes. Driver, runtimes, VM, plugin and conformance must pin the accepted contract independently; no consumer imports VM source as the protocol.

## Conformance vectors and actual verification

Require scalar/F64/QREG/TOKEN compatibility; aggregate padding and float-bit round trips; both concrete witnesses above; interior/overlapping/cyclic aliases; host edits between closed runs; free/reuse at the same address; null/one-past/range overflow; stale/wrong-program generations; conflicting leases; failed-store prefix and reverse; measurement ineligibility; returned host-owned allocations and native realloc; function pointers; two embedded roots; lifted private import and metadata-only invalidation. Compare the same materialised device closure and layout, preserving original pointer identity for address-sensitive baselines. Unregistered numeric CLI pointers and `--mem` hex copies are negative evidence controls. These are proposed integration vectors, not claims of an implemented backend. [INDEX](INDEX.md) records the actual bounded checks.

## K entry: 0.9 versus 0.10

Recommend a versioned companion `mixed-launch/memory` contract, normatively referenced from ISA 0.10 and the next dialect revision. A 0.9 K entry may clarify the existing by-value boundary, but cannot add pointer tags or memory semantics silently. Any in-version K route needs **council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules**, including negotiation and status encodings. Assign numeric K ids only on acceptance. No recipe catalog change is required by this design alone.

## Mixed fat object

Apply [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F04/F08/F23. Native main, argv/envp and host-only libc remain native; only marked modules and their complete device closures enter ISA inventory. Each root keeps its own certificate/context `[]`, budget/ledger and run/tape association. Shared storage uses explicit coordination across those roots. This keeps the mixed fat object reachable; it neither implements it nor makes its amendments prerequisites for rungs 3/4. F30 stays deferred.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

 succeeded in 0ms:
# Shared global state at native launch boundaries

Round-2 finding `bp-acm` requires a contract decision about the native caller
adapter. This document requests that decision; it does not adopt a new protocol
or claim that the bug is fixed.

Dialect 0.2.10 §4.3 requires one classical memory shared by H, CC, R and L
entry state. Section 19.4 preserves native H callers unless pointer-rich sharing
requires promotion. The mixed-object requirement `bennett-4ht` also preserves
ordinary native callers and multiple selected module entries.

The ISA 0.8 §10 C API supplies program/run ownership, typed arguments/results,
host requests, reversal and close/unload. Its published header has no operation
to bind, import or export a classical memory range. A layout image supplies
initial data, not shared native storage. Using a native pointer as an ISA
integer address does not connect the two memories.

The regression `tests/review_regressions.py --major M --case global-counter`
links the emitted host bitcode with the published VM library and tests three
observations on both LLVM 18 and 23:

| Native caller actions | Required output | Current output |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Call `top` twice | `1 2 host_global=2` | `1 1 host_global=0` |
| Call `top`, set native `g=40`, call again | `1 41 host_global=41` | `1 1 host_global=40` |
| Call `top`, then another marked entry sharing `g` | `1 2 host_global=2` | `1 1 host_global=0` |

The first row alone could tempt a program cache. A cache still leaves native
`g` unchanged and cannot import the intervening H write. Separate caches for
entries also retain separate device copies of the same source global. Neither
is a fix for §4.3. Promoting every native caller would change §19.4 placement.

The requested decision is the supported classical-state bridge for this
boundary: may the producer publish a versioned copy-in/writeback service using
existing ISA host requests, or should the launch contract supply explicit
memory-transfer/binding operations? Existing integer arguments/results could
also carry bytes through a generated adapter, but that would still need a
published mapping and ownership convention, not an unchecked host-pointer cast.

Whichever route is selected, its publication must specify:

- Source global identity, selected component ownership and native/device
  address correspondence, including globals shared by several entries.
- Copy-in of current H bytes and copy-out of device writes, including aliases,
  with the §19.4 promotion rule for pointer-rich reachable storage.
- Program context lifetime, compatible component linking, and handle/token
  namespaces; a function-local static cache cannot decide these for the native
  container.
- Memory ownership while a run is active, writeback of completed or faulted
  prefixes, and how reversal restores the host-visible state before subsequent
  H mutation or reuse.
- The adapter service/schema or C API, and the component metadata that lets
  plugin, driver, runtime and executor independently implement the same bridge.

This requests the smallest bridge needed to implement the existing shared
memory semantics. It does not request whole-process-only placement, private
access to VM memory, a change to the ISA 0.8 inner object grammar, or a silent
ISA upgrade. The general mixed-object audit is deferred; the determining
blocker here is the concrete P0 loss of an ordinary shared scalar global.

1-# Mixed fat-object lock-out audit
2-
3-Design audit for **bennett-4ht obligations 1–2**. No implementation or published contract is changed here. Build on the [shared scope](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md); that statement remains the common definition. The [08:45Z maintainer ruling][ruling] supplies the architectural constraint from [North Star §2][north-architecture], [§3.3][north-compilation] and [§4.4][north-ownership]. In particular, “whole-program” analysis must not become a requirement that the device object contain the whole native binary.
4-
5:The significant lock-outs are the missing ordinary-pointer launch/memory contract (F04/F08/F23), mandatory transitive promotion of pointer-sharing host code (F14), and the unqualified inventory of every link global (F11). The remaining rows distinguish missing associations and missing tests from compatible device-only restrictions. Current ladder success would not establish mixed-binary execution: the gates inspect host bitcode but drive device objects directly.

27-| F05 · bennett-isa-spec | [ISA §5.4][isa-budget], [§4.7][isa-calls], [§10.1][isa-estimate] | A validated tuple and token belong to a device program/run; nested calls retain their own tuple. An action call has no invented primary oracle token. | neutral | State: “Budgets and estimates bind selected device invocations; a native process has no implicit global B.” Include exact root/nested tuples and parameters in mixed-launch records. Account for device replay/wrappers under their existing units; report native/shim overhead separately. Keep token identity distinct from tuple equality and cache/claim binding. | Yes: scope and record design. |
28-| F06 · bennett-isa-spec | [ISA §3.3 ledger][isa-ledger], [§10 ownership][isa-launch] | Register ids and tokens resolve in a loaded program; touched resources are exclusive until CLOSED. Separate loaded objects do not automatically share a ledger. | makes harder | Define mixed-launch routing from an opaque host launch id to its owning program/ledger. Preserve identity across calls in that program. Cross-program use must have an explicit future ownership/transfer protocol or place the communicating device modules in one linked program; never reinterpret a bare id in another ledger or use a native pointer as the id. Specify stale/unload and wrong-owner diagnostics. | Yes for namespace/lifetime choice; transfer mechanics later. |
29-| F07 · bennett-isa-spec | [ISA §§7.3–7.4][isa-tape], [§10 tape API][isa-launch] | Active R tape is device-effect order; CC host replies are retained in run frames. Saved journal interchange is explicitly a versioned tool profile; tape_next returns dev/op/operand bytes, not a console-payload or native-process snapshot. | makes harder | Bind a mixed launch's journal to program/object/root, invocation, run and replay profile, with entropy/reply inputs and boundary ordering. Preserve separate R-effect, CC-reply and ordinary native-event ownership. Recovery must not issue external events again. Define native access to device-model outputs (including console payload bytes) in that profile; current tape operands alone do not export them. Keep the saved-journal envelope outside the active-tape API until versioned. | Yes: associations and replay design. |
30:| F08 · bennett-isa-spec | [ISA §§7.1–7.2][isa-memory], [§10 copied buffers][isa-launch], [§0.3 ISA-11/G-12][isa-deferred] | Device memory starts from layout images and bump-allocation metadata. Copying launch arguments and hostcall bytes provides no general live host-memory-by-reference binding. | locks out | Publish memory registration/mapping: range and target layout, pointer/address mapping, liveness and allocation metadata, alias preservation, ownership/synchronisation, read/write sets, writeback/visibility, reverse eligibility and release. Include pointer-rich graphs and host changes between calls. Retain snapshot/replay as one construction; promotion/copying must not be the sole eventual realization of native sharing. | Yes to name the exact contract now; runtime implementation is substantial. |
31:| F09 · bennett-isa-spec | [ISA §10 states][isa-launch], [§14.3 T4][isa-roundtrip] | Round trip restores the eligible **device run**, its active history and owned state; observed external events are not retracted, and measurement can make a run irreversible. | neutral | Add mixed-launch wording: reverse/close is per run, not rollback of native main, host libc, or all sibling device runs. Define the F08 memory writeback checkpoint and host-interference invalidation without weakening current ownership or measurement rules. | Yes: scope plus boundary protocol. |
32-| F10 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §§4.1–4.5][d2-tiers], [§19.1][d2-driver]; [0.3 same clauses][d3-tiers] [§19.1][d3-driver] | One merged LTO module is the analysis input; H originals and CC/R/L clones coexist. 33-| F11 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §19.5][d2-layout], especially `globals: every global of the link`; [0.3 §§4.3, 17.1–17.2][d3-tiers] [memory][d3-memory] [§19.5][d3-layout] | Every link global can become device layout state; shared globals and address identity are stated as one memory. 35-| F13 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §§6.1, 6.4, 6.6][d2-handles] [boundary][d2-boundary]; [0.3 counterparts][d3-handles] [boundary][d3-boundary]; [North Star §3.1][north-handles] | Source handle operations and measurement-dependent device work cannot survive as native host execution. 36:| F14 · bennett-dialect-spec | [0.2.12 §19.4][d2-placement]; [0.3 §19.4][d3-placement], [§25.3 ISA-11][d3-efficiency]; [ISA §0.3][isa-deferred] | Pointer-rich sharing **requires** H-to-CC promotion transitively to roots, potentially main. 54-
55-## ISA spec
56-
57-F01–F09 leave the inner ISA closed and independently verifiable. Native container fields belong to a separate versioned envelope, not ad hoc fields that current §9.1 must reject. Two embedded objects need not be flattened into one conflicting layout. If they actually import each other, §9.3 resolution, whole device-closure validation and certificate rebinding still apply.
58-
59-The certificate change is a scope statement plus an association, not a demand to create region records for native functions or every CC/R function. All records that the selected device closure actually requires remain mandatory. Separate device roots can reuse static callee code while retaining distinct invocation bindings and root-relative contexts. [Current §11 action-support text][isa-support] already states the desired native/device boundary for `$construct`/`$release`; generalise that good example to §§9/12/13 rather than claiming the entire published spec excludes fat objects.
--
84-
85-## Driver and runtimes (contract consumers)
86-
87-F21 is a driver publication task inferred from dialect §19.9 and North Star §4.4, not a source-level driver defect. ISA owns the exchanged format; the driver owns producing/consuming it. Container layout, shim ABI, root selection, versions and lifetime rules must be published before cross-repo implementation, rather than copied from another repository's source or inferred from `out/main` filenames.
88-
89:Runtime implementation follow-ups belong under the F08/F12/F17/F18 contracts: native startup/libc belongs to native main, device process context only to a requested whole-device mode, and R/L effect models remain distinct from host I/O. No new runtime implementation bead is proposed independently of those decisions; the orchestrator should attach owning implementation work when the versioned contract is accepted.
90-
91-## Conformance ladder
92-
93:F26–F30 identify a coverage gap, not a reason to weaken existing acceptance. Rung 3 deliberately demonstrates an entire reversible musl program; rung 4 deliberately requires the real SQLite device closure and fixed database domain. Their strict libc, tape, domain, shape and evidence-label checks stay intact. Rungs 1/2 also retain their current domains and reversal checks. Rungs 5–8 have acceptance READMEs rather than an inspected mixed implementation gate; no undocumented mixed acceptance is inferred from them.
94-
95:The first amendment is an **additional** mixed execution test with its own status and evidence. File F30 when rung 3 or 4 lands, as requested by bennett-4ht; it is not a new prerequisite to either rung. Lack of a published mixed ABI must appear as an explicit dependency, never a reason to reinterpret a passing whole-device rung as a passing mixed rung. The proposed first scalar fixture witnesses only the native/device split; pointer-rich memory sharing, cross-launch resources and larger applications remain explicit obligations.
96-

Each note cites the shared [fat-object scope](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [audit](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md). Same-version ISA K routes always require council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules; otherwise semantic changes route to 0.10. No git command or full gate is part of this job.

## Dispositions and publication boundary

Sixteen grouped notes cover 28 rows; two rows cite complete existing derivations. The actual pou record requests exactly the zero-low PPC tie repair already derived in [4yg.1](../2026-11-02-derive3/bennett-arith-4yg.1.md). The actual q06 record requests exactly the runtime all-width request/manifest/fixed-point protocol in [brt-vbi](../2026-11-02-derive3/brt-vbi.md). They need no duplicate proposal or ISA K. This pass confirms their scope against the actual issue records; it does not rerun derive3's native arithmetic or LLVM runtime-body audits. Their prior execution results remain labelled prior evidence. Both dispositions are amendments, never prerequisites for rungs 3/4, and F30 remains deferred.

F23 has an existing [VM design proposal](../../../../bennett-vm/docs/PLAN.md), not a published ABI or implemented backend. The native-sharing note derives the contract obligations it must consume. Existing 0jq/7s0/djm and shared-scope notes overlap inventory, frame proof and process preparation, but do not resolve the new owner, cross-region adjoint, callable-entry or native-memory protocols. They are cited rather than rewritten. F31's boundary-preservation inference is retained as an associated obligation in device-closure, without adding a bead to the requested 30-row index or claiming an observed inliner bug.

All source issues remain open for their publication/implementation owners. This notes-only job has completed the derivations; it does not close amendments that have not been ratified or implemented. No spec, header, catalog, interpreter or sibling source was edited. The runner owns commits; no commit SHA is asserted here.

## Evidence and bounded verification

[sources.json](sources.json) preserves all 30 actual issue descriptions/titles/notes and nine cited source snapshots with SHA-256 values and exact excerpts. ISA records were read with `bd --readonly show --json` from the main checkout. The required direct dialect/VM reads were attempted from their main checkouts and failed on read-only embedded Dolt LOCK/gate files. Copying each actual embedded database plus its metadata/config into `.tmp/sol-derive4/` permitted `bd --readonly show --json` there. No passive JSONL import, sibling tracker mutation or remote sync was used. Source snapshots are evidence pins; the verifier reports concurrent source drift separately from its frozen excerpts.

The job-built reference interpreter was compiled under a **4 GiB address-space limit**, offline with two build jobs. Environment for all commands below:

Observed exit 0. The script additionally enforces its own 1 GiB limit and 15-second deadline on each reference subprocess. Results:

| Check | Observed result and limit |
|---|---|
| Index/notes/citations | 30 unique rows; 16 grouped notes; two prior dispositions; all required sections, links, fences, fat-object/R10/publication rules pass |
| Frozen evidence | 30 issue records and nine source snapshots pass; zero source drift on the verification run |
| Native boundary witnesses | Finite alias/epoch, metadata invalidation, wrong-ledger/generation, lease-conflict, payload-integrity, device-closure, root-context, token-value/identity and entry-role counterexamples pass; these are protocol models, not a VM/compiler implementation |
| Class/borrow/adjoint | Capacity/default-class distinction, occupancy 14 versus 10, all eight forward and reversed four-CX columns and B-dependent phase counterexample pass; no scheduler/checker C ABI probe rerun |
| qjumpr candidate | Actual published recipe transformed in memory; 1,216 bounded P/A/First/End/address/initial-successor cases match full-width predicates, XOR/fault writes and exact inverse/scratch restoration |
| Return binding | Complete scan width exceeds old N=4; W1=8 changes history 17 to 21 bits and admits every named scan bit; this is width/arithmetic evidence, not full lift.ret expansion conformance |
| Reference source objects | `check`/identity validates both read-only estimator fixtures and the new diagonal fixture |
| Reference return fixture | `run-oracle .../ret-delivery-source-gap.bisa root --input '' --budget 4,1,8,1`: payload 1, code 0, steps 4 |
| Reference jump fixture | `run-oracle .../jump-width-source-gap.bisa root --input 1,2,1 --budget 3,0,0,0`: payload 1, code 0, steps 3 |
| Reference diagonal fixture | Four basis calls preserve t/x; forward and adjoint on `00=0.6;01=0.8` preserve the complete amplitudes; both reverse calls report REVERSED, equals_entry=true, no first difference |

The candidate qjumpr recipe hashes to `bf4e2d395f642cdb645184c18650e867c614edc259ff9f5e74f29047d00acd1c` on the witness's canonical JSON domain. This is a recipe candidate fingerprint, **not** a published catalog/envelope hash. The [diagonal fixture](diagonal-source.bisa) has object_hash `cabcde923d3c5b8a66398caf34778ca64c799ea90c9e7b86d7d5be09b8a559bb` and interface/closure hash `8eb4012028bd0aa8b5ecccfd89b72f1ae46be842e4f421ec0502e04f656751b5`.

One corrected evidence detail: gck's lowercase `z` shorthand is not a §4.4 opcode. The initial reference attempt rejected it. The admitted fixture uses forward S² and inverse SDG², retaining the exact I-versus-unwanted-Z counterexample. This is a witness normalization, not an interpreter repair or a claim that the schematic spelling was admitted source.

These checks establish the stated finite witnesses and document coverage. They do not establish a general cleanup-segmentation theorem, an implemented native container/adapter, live registered-memory sharing, a production certificate schema, a complete points-to analysis or end-to-end mixed execution. Those remain explicit amendment/implementation work, with all North Star capability domains preserved. F30 remains deferred; existing rungs retain their original acceptance.
