## Host memory binding design, proposal 0.1 (bennett-vm-8v0)

This is a VM design and integration-vector specification for audit F23, not a
published launch ABI or an implemented backend. Publication and implementation
must follow ISA F04 (`b-isa-spec-x1y`), ISA F08 (`b-isa-spec-6ma`), dialect F15
(`b-dialect-spec-5dl`) and dialect F16 (`b-dialect-spec-dce`). Their passive
issue exports still report all four open. The accepted ISA 0.8 and 0.9
by-value interfaces retain their existing signatures, tags and behavior.

The boundary follows the [mixed-object scope](../../bennett-isa-spec/docs/notes/2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md)
and [F23 audit](../../bennett-isa-spec/docs/notes/2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md).
A native host binary keeps its own entry, argv/envp, libc and unrelated globals.
A launch chooses one embedded device program, root and certificate; neither its
object hash nor its certificate inventory denotes the whole native binary.
Native and device functions can share registered storage without promoting
native main or every pointer-sharing caller into the device program.

### Current boundary and proposed extension

The argument variants in [launch.rs](../src/launch.rs) are Int/F64/Qreg/Token;
`bennett_run` has no ordinary Ptr binding. `Resources::new` and `Data::new` in
[cc.rs](../src/cc.rs) create device state from the ISA layout, images and
allocation table. A CLI numeric pointer is an address in that device model.
Copied argument buffers, hostcall payloads and hex memory images do not register
a live native allocation. These are interface observations, not a reproduced
failure of an existing native-sharing implementation.

The proposed extension has its own negotiated profile/version and explicit
registration and invocation objects. Symbol names, numeric tags, C structure
layouts and status values remain for F04/F08 publication; this proposal adds
none to current headers or exports. An unsupported version fails negotiation
before a run or lease is created. A new pointer argument is a registered-memory
capability plus an offset, with validated native/device address and layout
information; an integer argument alone never grants native access. Scalar and
aggregate argument/result marshalling uses the published target ABI, including
field offsets, pointer kinds, padding policy, widths, byte order and floating
point bit patterns. It does not guess those properties from host Rust types.

| Record | Required content and invariant |
|---|---|
| Registration | Allocation owner, canonical allocation identity, generation, base/extent, permissions, liveness authority, target layout, memory/backend profile and allocator policy. Registration is a lifetime protocol, not a claim that a numeric address is readable. |
| Program binding | Registration identity/generation mapped into one loaded device program and its layout. Distinct embedded programs keep distinct ledgers; a bare id from program A is invalid in B. A common registration coordinator arbitrates leases on their shared native storage. |
| Pointer slot | Registration capability and checked offset, including null/one-past rules, plus the declared data/function/opaque pointer kind. The marshalled Ptr bits must satisfy the selected mapping profile. |
| Invocation binding | Selected object/root/certificate identities, resolved arguments, layout/mapping version, registration generations and entry epochs, run ownership and replay profile. Runtime addresses belong here, not in immutable ISA-object bytes. |
| Lease/checkpoint | Read-only or exclusive read/write range ownership, entry allocation metadata, entry memory version, and the run's ordered write/allocation history. Unload, release and reverse respect this owner. |

A registration accepts runtime-owned storage or an allocation whose owner can
hold its lifetime and supply the published liveness/lease contract. Extent checks
alone cannot establish that arbitrary C storage remains alive. C callers must
honor that contract; an owner hook, safe-language borrow or an accepted platform
mechanism supplies it without requiring the host code to become ISA code.
A generation prevents stale capabilities from becoming valid after address reuse.

### Address identity, aliases and allocators

The initial proposed profile preserves native pointer bits for registered
storage when native and target pointer width, representation and layout agree.
Interior pointers are one allocation identity plus offsets. Overlapping argument
ranges retain one underlying storage identity and one coordinated lease; writes
through one permitted alias are visible through the others. A second copied
buffer is not an alias implementation. Source `noalias` obligations, when present
in the accepted binding description, remain obligations rather than a blanket
ban on legitimate aliased Ptr arguments.

Bounds and nonwrapping address arithmetic are checked before converting an ISA
address to a host-sized offset. A registered range cannot silently overlap the
null area, a different live allocation or device-private storage. Shared globals
have an explicit common binding. Unrelated native globals stay outside device
layout and proof inventory. F16 must publish the association and relocation rules
for other layouts; silent pointer swizzling, changed pointer comparisons/hashes,
or copying unidentified integer words as pointers are not substitutes.
Function pointers require an explicit native-address-to-device-dispatch mapping;
registering data bytes grants no permission to call native code from a device
closure. Existing CC hostcall and device callback/re-entry rules stay separate.

Device-private bump allocations retain their current semantics. A native-visible
escaping allocation needs an accepted host-compatible allocation/transfer profile
covering native free/realloc, aliases, allocation-table metadata, budgets and
reverse history. Mapping a private device integer into a returned native pointer
is not that profile. F08/F15 must specify how allocator and shadow-stack entry
state is imported and how escaping storage becomes host-owned. This explicitly
retains pointer-rich allocation and native ownership in scope; caller-provided
borrowed buffers are not the final capability boundary.

### Backend access and coherent execution

The implementation boundary is a checked memory backend shared by the interpreter
and JIT. Device-owned sparse memory remains one backend. A future registered-host
backend resolves an address to a validated registration, generation and offset,
checks its lease/liveness/permissions, and only then performs a bounded access.
There is no native pointer dereference in today's integer-address backend.
JIT memory operations must use the same checks or an accepted guarded lease/epoch
path, with invalidation on release or rebinding; cached integer bits do not carry
a lifetime proof. No run-wide atomicity is inferred from one checked load/store.

CC/R classical memory sharing and coherent device memory are distinct storage
kinds. A lifted/oracle construction imports a consistent, registered entry image
and equivalent allocation/shadow-stack metadata before private allocations, then
uses its prescribed private forward/reverse machine. It does not perform an
observable native RAM load at a quantum address or expose unmeasured coherent
state as C bytes. Existing snapshot/replay remains an admitted construction;
F08 must also provide the live native-sharing route, with explicit conversions
where needed, rather than require universal host promotion. A numeric CLI image
or a successful snapshot replay is not evidence of a live host binding.

### Visibility, reversal and effects

The proposed mutable profile takes an exclusive lease before execution and holds
it through run close; compatible read-only leases can coexist. Native mutation,
free/realloc, a conflicting launch and unregister cannot proceed while that lease
is held. Writes may already reside in the registered pages during execution;
visibility is synchronized at the lease boundary for its participants, not an
assertion of hardware-atomic publication of an arbitrary memory graph. Hashing
bytes once cannot detect every unsynchronized raw write. The accepted owner and
interference protocol must state which accesses it excludes or detects.

A synchronous native adapter waits for completion, obtains results, then commits
and closes before returning control that may access the shared storage. While a
completed eligible run still owns its lease, reverse restores that run's entry
bytes, aliases, allocation metadata and ownership history before close. It never
rolls back native main, host-only libc, a sibling run or host edits made before
this invocation. Each new launch observes the current native state and records a
new entry epoch; replay cannot reuse an earlier launch's snapshot silently.

A failing access changes no state for that operation. Earlier valid writes remain
part of the failed run's prefix and its journal. Eligible reversal restores that
prefix; an irreversible failure must report its actual committed prefix when the
published close policy releases ownership. Measurement and other existing reverse
restrictions still apply. Stale/revoked storage or detected interference fails
before a native access or reverse write, through negotiated device/launch status,
not an unchecked pointer access or a process-aborting assertion. F04/F08 must
publish these state/visibility rules and status encodings before implementation.

The journal association includes the selected device run/root, binding and entry
epoch. Device R effects and CC host replies keep their existing ordering and
exactly-once replay rules. Ordinary native I/O is not silently added to the R tape,
and reverse does not retract observed external events. Budgets and estimates bind
that device invocation and its actual replay work; the native process acquires no
implicit global budget. Host registration/shim costs need separate reporting, not
invented oracle units. Quantum-register ids and token identities stay in their
own program/run namespaces and are never reinterpreted as native pointers.

### Bounded integration vectors for the accepted implementation

These are planned acceptance vectors, not executed tests or claims that the
backend exists. Each vector must run through interpreter and JIT after the four
contracts are published. A native comparison uses the same selected materialised
closure, target layout and entry allocation graph; sequential baseline restoration
must preserve pointer identity when comparing pointer-valued or address-hashed
results. It does not compare a whole native process against one device root.

| Vector | Fixture and determining observations |
|---|---|
| HMB-01 by-value compatibility | Run the existing scalar, float-bit, qreg and token launch corpus unchanged. Compare statuses, results, tapes, resource ownership and reverse outcomes; the new profile is not selected. |
| HMB-02 host edits between calls | Register four bytes `[10,20,30,40]`. First launch adds 3 to byte 1 and closes, exposing `[10,23,30,40]`. Native code writes byte 0 to 99. The next launch XORs byte 0 with 1; reverse before close restores `[99,23,30,40]`, not the first launch's baseline. Assert distinct invocation entry epochs. |
| HMB-03 pointer graph and interior aliases | Two nodes link cyclically and one field points to the other's value. Two arguments denote the same interior value. Write 7 through one and read 7 through the other; compare pointer equality and address-derived outputs with the native closure. Reverse restores original bytes, links, allocation identity and liveness. |
| HMB-04 overlapping views | Two registered views of an 8-byte allocation overlap. Verify one canonical owner, shared updates, correct strongest lease, read-only-write failure before the store, and unchanged sentinels outside the views. Apply `noalias` checks only when the accepted signature requires them. |
| HMB-05 liveness and representation | Exercise null, one-past, zero-length, range overflow, native/target width or layout mismatch, unregister and free/reuse at the same address. Stale generations and unregistered numeric Ptr bits fail before access; no sentinel native memory is touched. |
| HMB-06 interference and program ownership | While a run owns a mutable lease, a cooperating host write/free/unregister and a second program's overlapping launch are denied or invalidate exactly as the published profile specifies. Verify the documented diagnostic before mutation. Wrong-program capabilities fail; compatible disjoint/read-only leases exercise the permitted paths. |
| HMB-07 partial failure and reverse | A valid first store changes byte 0 from 3 to 7; a subsequent access to an unregistered range faults without touching that range. Assert one fault record and the exact retained prefix. Eligible reverse restores 3 before close; exercise the separately published irreversible-close path as well. |
| HMB-08 effects and reverse eligibility | Combine a registered write with an R effect/CC reply, then a measurement case. Verify device journal association, no duplicated effect/reply, the existing irreversible decision and the exact visible bytes at close. Native-only I/O remains a separate observation. |
| HMB-09 escaping allocation | Under the accepted host-compatible allocator profile, create and return a node linked into the registered graph. Native code can use and free/realloc it after close. Reversal before publication restores the graph and allocator metadata. Test interior aliases, stale generations and an attempted private-device-pointer escape. |
| HMB-10 lifted entry import | Import the bytes and allocation/shadow-stack metadata of a small registered graph at one epoch before the oracle's private allocations. Check payload/fault, private-memory restoration and unchanged host `in` storage in both engines. A later host edit changes the next entry image. This is snapshot evidence, distinct from live CC/R sharing. |
| HMB-11 mixed native binary | Native main calls two marked roots in separate embedded objects, retaining a native-only dependency and unrelated globals. Check per-root identities/certificates/budgets, shared registration coordination, strict wrong-ledger rejection and independent reverse/close. No native main/argv/libc certificate or implicit process budget is invented. |

# Bennett ISA reference interpreter

Rust 2021, standard library only. ISA 0.8 remains supported; ISA 0.9 migration
is in progress. Complete ISA 0.9 execution is not yet claimed. [PROGRESS.md](PROGRESS.md) records
what is verified, what is staged and in what order; [SPEC-QUESTIONS.md](SPEC-QUESTIONS.md)
records every reading the specification leaves open;
[council/response-round-1.md](council/response-round-1.md) answers the council's
round-1 review blocker by blocker. The crate is not yet a complete conformance
reference: the launch ABI (§10), target descriptions (§8), the schedule
consumer (§11) and the rigorous interval certificate checker (§12.1) are staged
as deliverables 8–11, and claims that need them are reported as unverified,
never as verified.

Build and test offline from the repository root (headless: `DISPLAY`,
`WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS`, `BROWSER` unset;
`TMPDIR=$PWD/reference-interpreter/.tmp`, created first):

```sh
export CARGO_TARGET_DIR="$PWD/reference-interpreter/.tmp/build"
cargo build --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline
python3 -I reference-interpreter/tools/publish-cli.py \
  "$CARGO_TARGET_DIR/debug/bennett-isa-ref"
cargo test --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline
cargo test --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline -- --ignored
```

The published executable is `reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref`.
The publisher copies a completed private build and atomically replaces that
path with a regular executable snapshot. Concurrent consumers can continue
using it while Cargo replaces its private build output. Publish only after a
successful build; jobs may supply their own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`.
Data
commands write one JSON object to stdout and exit 0; `help`/`--help` prints
plain usage. Diagnostics go to stderr with exit status 1, and ISA diagnostics
cite their rule. Integers above 2^53 − 1 are decimal strings (§9.1).

> **Council status (2026-10-31):** accepted by the sol and codex seats in round 24 of run `isa-ref-2026-10-29` on snapshot ad84e75 (461 tests, five exhaustive sweeps; rounds 1–24 under `bennett/docs/council/isa-ref-2026-10-29/`). Staged deliverables 8–11 remain disclosed as staged. Muse seat held.

## Typed R faults and contract migration (ISA-7)

ISA 0.9 already resolves b-isa-spec-221: §0.2 K5, §4.1 `fault`,
§4.9 (implicit cause table, exact 64-bit terminal code, no successor or
later source effect, intact prefix history), §7.4, §10 (FAILED → REVERSED,
no result values, one fault record) and §14.3 T4. Zero maps to code 4;
nonzero codes, including program-defined codes, are preserved exactly.
This is a published 0.9 contract, not an amendment to accepted ISA 0.8.

The dialect 0.2.10 at `023f5a7` §11.5/§17.6 correctly reports
`blocked:BD-B-ISA7` for every R entry targeting ISA 0.8: the mandatory
pre-instruction source step check needs `fault 1` before the instruction,
with the same effect-tape prefix and reversible history. `--budget` here
is host execution fuel, not that source counter; generic `trap` cannot
implement arbitrary typed R faults. Closing the ISA wording issue does
not clear that consumer blocker or certify rung 3. Open follow-up
`b-isa-spec-fuk` retains that producer/executor migration prerequisite.

The reference migration has landed explicit 0.8/0.9 envelope, ABI, identity,
linking and device-version dispatch, plus K5 typed terminal faults and eligible
FAILED-run reversal on 0.9. `tests/typed_faults.rs` checks source-instrumented
pre-instruction boundaries (zero and returns included), exact uint64 codes,
implicit causes, intact prefixes and entry restoration. The source counter
remains producer instrumentation; host fuel is a separate diagnostic.

Compatibility note (`b-isa-spec-0u1`): the interpreter's 0.8 mode already
admitted the address-only `()` actual for `region[0,W]` before this migration.
That historical extension is preserved; the published 0.8 contract is unchanged.
`tests/memory_address.rs` retains its existing 0.8 regressions, while
`tests/isa09_memory_address.rs` checks actual 0.9 source expansion and native
execution against K79, including zero-width addresses.

Bound `ref` expansion, end-to-end K79–K81 execution, the 0.9 saved-tape matrix
and the remaining authoritative delta (including dynamic qregs, minted tokens,
parametrised qinplace and nonempty lproc forwarding) remain open under
`b-isa-spec-fuk.3`–`.7`. The producer and sibling contract pins have not been
migrated here. The full musl hello-world, native equality, R console, reset
and saved-tape replay acceptance remains owed (North Star §8 rung 3;
dialect §19.2). The council status above applies only to its pinned snapshot;
a new milestone acceptance round remains the orchestrator's responsibility.

## Commands

`check <object>` parses and validates the object, computes identities, and
verifies every stored interface hash. Output: `object_hash`, `status`
(`"ok"`, or `"unlinked"` when imports are unresolved), `symbols` (each with
`interface_hash` and `closure_hash`, null when its closure has an unpinned
import) and `unresolved_imports`.

`hash <object>` produces the same identities without comparing stored hashes
(for building fixtures; it edits nothing).

`run-oracle <object> <oracle> --input <x[,y...]> [options]` executes the §14.1
interpreter and prints `{"code":0,"payload":9,"steps":3}` for inc8(8) at B=3.
Inputs of any width are decimal or `0x` hexadecimal; `--input ""` is the empty
list. The oracle name may start with `@`.

Options:

- `--budget B` or `--budget B,heap,stack,depth`. Default B is 1,000,000.
  Layout limits supply heap and stack defaults; depth defaults to 64 with a
  layout; memoryless limits are zero.
- `--mem name=hex` supplies an interface region's bytes in cell order;
  unspecified regions start at zero; the byte count must be exact.
- `--arg value` supplies the next classical argument: an integer,
  `f64:0.5`, or `token:B,heap,stack,depth` (after `--budget` the abbreviated
  token takes its limits as defaults).

`step-check <object> <oracle> MODE [options]` runs the independent §14.1
interpreter and the literal §3.6 machine on each domain element, compares their
F_B, then runs the B inverse steps and compares every location, cell, counter,
stack cell, allocator entry and frame with the entry tuple (T2, T6). The domain
is every input bundle and every byte of every interface region not fixed by
`--mem`; target and fault entry values do not affect the computation (XOR
delivery). MODE is one of:

- `--exhaustive [--max-bits N]`: the whole domain, refusing domains above the
  user's guard N (default 16; no fixed ceiling);
- `--suite`: the §17 item 13 default, exhaustive at ≤ 16 bits and otherwise
  2^16 xoshiro256** samples;
- `--samples N`: N samples, N ≥ 1 (`--seed a,b,c,d` sets the four state words).

    pub fn initial(
        object: &Object,
        oracle: &Function,
        regions: &BTreeMap<String, Vec<u8>>,
    ) -> Result<Self> {
        let expected = if oracle.kind == "oracle" {
            oracle.params[3].len()
        } else {
            0
        };
        if regions.len() != expected {
            return err("§5.1", "interface region count mismatch");
        }
        let Some(layout) = object.section("layout") else {
            return Ok(Self::default());
        };
        let mut m = Self {
            heap_base: layout.get("heap_base")?.uint()?,
            table_capacity: power_of_two(layout.get("A_tab")?.uint()?),
            ..Self::default()
        };
        // Pre-entries in layout order: globals, then this oracle's regions (§3.7).
        for (n, g) in layout.get("globals")?.object()? {
            let base = g.get("base")?.uint()?;
            let size = g.get("size")?.uint()?;
            m.addresses.insert(n.clone(), base);
            m.allocations.push(Allocation {
                base,
                size,
                live: true,
                pinned: true,
            });
            let image = object
                .data
                .get(n)
                .ok_or_else(|| Error::new(format!("§9.2: global {n} has no data image")))?;
            for (i, b) in image.iter().enumerate() {
                m.set_byte(offset(base, i)?, *b)?;
            }
        }
        if oracle.kind == "oracle" && !oracle.params[3].is_empty() {
            let mapping = layout.get("interface_regions")?.get(&oracle.name)?;
            for (n, r) in mapping.object()? {
                let p = oracle.params[3]
                    .iter()
                    .find(|p| p.name == *n)
                    .ok_or_else(|| Error::new(format!("§7.2: layout maps unknown region {n}")))?;
                let Type::Region(a, _) = p.ty else {
                    return err("§5.1", "mem slot requires region type");
                };
                let base = r.get("base")?.uint()?;
                let size = power_of_two(a as u128)
                    .ok_or_else(|| Error::new("§7.2: interface region exceeds 2^H".into()))?;
                let bytes = regions
                    .get(n)
                    .ok_or_else(|| Error::new(format!("§5.1: missing interface region {n}")))?;

# F18 — copied outbound services and registered inbound calls

Bead: **b-dialect-spec-3ca**. Amendment proposal, never a prerequisite for rungs 3/4; F30 remains deferred until rung 3 or 4 lands.

## Exact defect and clause citations

[Dialect 0.2.12](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md) and [0.3](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md) §11.4 describe CC-to-H scalar/pointer-free copied service arguments and writeback, with pointer-rich callees promoted. [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md) §§7.4/10 retain request/reply history and validate a copied live service range. Neither is an inbound native-pointer registration or a callback/reentry ownership contract. §11.4 also lacks a complete alias policy for overlapping copied pointees. See [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F18.

## North Star gap

Direction/ownership publication, not authority to infer a live pointer from a service byte block. Native-sharing capability remains required under F04/F08 for North Star §§3.3/4.4. The existing copied CC path and R/L effect restrictions remain valid within their domain.

## Alternatives and witnesses

Device CC sends a four-byte pointer-free pointee to an H service. The host stub writes 7 to its copy; only the declared reply/writeback updates device memory. This grants no live access to the caller's arbitrary native graph. Two overlapping pointee views must share one copied backing range if the service can observe aliasing; independent copies can return the wrong result. A callback into a second run touching the first run's still-leased range conflicts even while the first is HOST_REQUEST. Prefer copied-range alias preservation plus an explicit future registered/reentry profile, rather than silently releasing ownership during host service.

## Recommended exact dialect diff

Append to §11.4:

```text
This is an outbound CC service protocol. Its pointer-free proof, explicit
extent, scalar encoding, copy ownership and reply/writeback bounds do not
register native storage or implement inbound native pointer arguments.
The service stub may use only its copied ranges for that request; request
payload lifetime is not a general host allocation lifetime. Preserve full
reply length/truncation and before-mutation range checks of ISA §10.

For pointer-free aliased arguments, normalize overlapping views of one
device allocation into a common copied backing range, preserve their
relative offsets and alias observations, and write back each covered byte
once under a specified consistent reply. The versioned service-block
profile records that range/view map; it does not infer aliasing from two
independent byte arrays. Independent/disjoint copies remain valid when
the analysis proves their observations equivalent. Native code must not
retain a service-copy pointer after reply. Pointer-rich native sharing
uses the separately published registration profile, not silent promotion
as the only eventual realization (§19.4).

HOST_REQUEST does not close the run or release its device/registered-memory
ownership. A service callback/reentry profile records parent run/request,
program/root, ordering and exact permitted leases. Reentry with disjoint
state may use an independent run under existing ownership rules. Conflicting
shared state requires a published atomic ownership delegation/handoff:
quiesce the parent, record exact ranges/generations and owner chain, prevent
parent access while delegated, restore ownership on reply/failure, and bind
all child history/results before resuming. Without that protocol the
conflict is diagnosed before mutation, not bypassed by a hidden unlock.
This names required future mechanics, not a source-level callback ban.

Retain R effect/CC reply/native event separation and source order. Recovery
uses retained replies without reissuing the service or callback. Reversing
the device buffer/history does not retract external host effects. R/L
remain closed to genuine hostcalls; registration does not change their
tier rules or permit native handle operations.
```

The new alias-range service encoding and any shared callback handoff need a versioned publication before consumers emit them. No new numeric service tag or current C API is introduced by this proposal.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Service declaration/adapter/body changes can change device object/closure/build hashes. Per-request copied bytes, range maps, replies and parent/child ownership bind invocation/journal records. They are not native pointer identities. No ref catalog change follows from documenting direction. Driver/plugin/runtime/VM/replay consumers must pin a published service/profile encoding rather than share implementation code.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Require pointer-free copy/writeback/truncation; aliasing overlapping views versus disjoint ranges; no retained pointer; bad range/reply before mutation; pointer-rich path using accepted registration; disjoint reentry; conflicting lease rejected before mutation; future handoff success/failure ownership restoration; exactly-once retained replies; R/L no genuine hostcalls. The finite model in [verify.py](verify.py) checks alias and lease counterexamples only; actual host-service execution is not claimed in [INDEX](INDEX.md).

## K entry: 0.9 versus 0.10

Next maintained dialect revision plus 0.3 draft; new service/registration/callback profiles default to companion/**0.10** integration. Existing ISA copied hostcall semantics are preserved. An in-version 0.9 K route needs **council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules**, including service-block alias encoding and old consumer negotiation. Proposed decision `MIXED-HOSTCALL-DIRECTION-AND-REENTRY`.

## Mixed fat object

Apply [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F18. Native main/libc remain native; only marked device closures receive certificates/context `[]`, budgets and ledger ownership. Requests/journals bind their selected device run; native-memory sharing requires explicit registration, not whole-binary promotion. These amendments never gate rungs 3/4; F30 stays deferred.
# F14 — retain native pointer-sharing callers

Bead: **b-dialect-spec-yqf**. Amendment proposal, never a prerequisite for rungs 3/4; F30 remains deferred until rung 3 or 4 lands.

## Exact defect and clause citations

Both [dialect 0.2.12](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md) and [0.3 draft](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md) §19.4 require pointer-sharing H functions to be promoted transitively, potentially through main. §11.4 also calls for promotion of a pointer-rich outbound callee. The 0.3 §25.3 row classifies ISA-11 as an efficiency item satisfied by promotion. That preserves device computation but not a native host around marked modules. The §25.1 statement retiring five device-lowering blockers does not publish a mixed memory ABI. See [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F14.

## North Star gap

Yes, for the selective-module experience in North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4. Promotion remains a valid construction; making it the permanent mandatory realization excludes the required native-sharing case. This is a boundary publication obligation, not a reason to reopen retired device-lowering blockers or block rungs 3/4.

## Alternatives and witnesses

Native main owns cyclic nodes A.next=B and B.next=A, passes two aliases of B.value to marked update, then uses its native-only library. Promotion through main loses that split. Copying each argument independently loses alias visibility. A versioned registration of the graph, preserving pointer observations and lifetime, lets update write 7 through either alias and main observe 7 after close. Retaining native main while silently using old device images fails when it changes B.value between calls.

Prefer explicit mixed-launch/memory profiles from [F04/F08/F23](native-sharing.md). Universal promotion is a supported fallback only when that mode is selected. A scalar-only mixed fixture is useful evidence but cannot discharge this pointer-rich obligation. The graph/epoch finite checks in [verify.py](verify.py) distinguish these alternatives; no compiler/native-sharing execution is claimed.

## Recommended exact dialect diff

Replace §19.4's paragraph in the next maintained revision and mirror in 0.3:

```text
Host placement is a mode choice with preserved source semantics. In the
promotion construction, H functions sharing pointer-rich memory with
device code have CC variants, transitively to their selected roots, as
in the existing construction. This construction remains supported.

Mixed native-sharing mode instead retains native callers and binds their
shared storage under the published mixed-launch/memory profile referenced
by the selected container. It preserves aliases, pointer observations,
liveness, allocation metadata and shared-global identity, and defines
ownership, synchronisation, writeback and reverse/close. Promotion cannot
be the only eventual realization of this mode. A missing implementation
is tracked against that contract, not reported as a source rejection or
silently satisfied by promoting native main. Until the profile is
published this is an explicit publication obligation, not an implemented
driver option or permission to improvise an ABI.
```

Replace the ISA-11 row of §25.3 with:

```text
ISA-11 | promotion realizes device computation; retaining pointer-sharing
native callers additionally requires the mixed-launch/memory contract
(ISA F04/F08; dialect F11/F15/F16). This is a boundary capability
obligation, not merely a cost optimization of mixed mode.
```

Append to §25.1's introduction:

```text
Retirement of the listed device-lowering blockers does not assert that
native-container packaging, native function adapters or live host-memory
sharing are already published. Those mixed-boundary amendments do not
change the acceptance of current device-only rungs.
```

Append to §11.4's pointer-rich promotion sentence: `This describes the promotion construction of §19.4; a future native-sharing realization uses the explicitly published profile and callback/ownership rules, never the copied service buffer alone.`

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Placement can change device closure membership, emitted wrappers and object/certificate/build identities. A selected memory profile and runtime mapping are invocation bindings; they do not replace device closure hashes with a host hash. No `ref@0.9` catalog edit follows from this classification. Driver/plugin/runtime/VM must coordinate a published profile and independently pinned versions, including [F11](b-dialect-spec-hsb.md)'s shared-state closure.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Require native main plus a native-only dependency, cyclic/interior aliases, shared globals, host mutation between closed calls and exact writeback/reverse. Inspect both native artifact and selected ISA inventories; promotion-only output must be labelled as such. Retain the old promotion vector and all device faults/coverage. Test unsupported profile negotiation before effects, rather than introducing a program-domain rejection. Actual checks are the finite model and citation/structure checks in [INDEX](INDEX.md); a compiler or backend implementation is outside this note.

## K entry: 0.9 versus 0.10

No ISA K is needed to correct the dialect's classification. Publish a new maintained dialect revision with matching 0.3 text; preserve old pins. Its actual native-sharing ABI follows the new companion contract, default ISA 0.10 route. An ISA 0.9 K route requires **council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules**. The proposed dialect decision is `MIXED-PLACEMENT: promotion is one realization; native-sharing publication remains required`.

## Mixed fat object

Use [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F14. Only marked modules and their required device closures enter ISA objects; native main/startup/argv/envp and host-only libc retain native ownership. Per-root certificates/contexts, budgets, handles and tape associations remain separate. This amendment directly removes an architectural lock-out without claiming the future ABI exists. It is never a prerequisite for rungs 3/4; F30 stays deferred.
reach distinct instances, each using its own fixed arguments. This is
the reference semantics; executors MAY share their storage or inline
instances only extensionally (§5.7).

Each instance declares its own `regs`. All locations are clean at entry.
The oracle's `result_val : wire[T]` (T = the `tgt` width) is step state,
initially 0.

**Counters and sizes.** Size exponents: `mem` 2^H byte cells, `alloctab`
2^A_tab entries, stack 2^S_cells cells. Counter widths: `heap_used :
wire[Hc]`, Hc ≥ H + 2; `atop : wire[Ac]`, Ac ≥ A_tab + 1; `sp, fp :
wire[Sc]`, Sc ≥ S_cells + 1; `depth : wire[Dc]`, Dc ≥ ⌈log₂(depth_limit +
2)⌉. Every prospective sum (`heap_used + max(n,1)`, `sp + R_f`, `depth +
1`) is computed at width max(operand widths) + 1 before comparison, with
no wrap. `bennett_budget` rejects limits that do not fit (§5.4). The
layout fixes no counter width: the reference realisation uses the
minimum widths Hc = H + 2, Ac = A_tab + 1, Sc = S_cells + 1 and Dc =
⌈log₂(depth_limit + 2)⌉ (Dc from the invocation's budget). Every reachable
counter value fits them (heap_used ≤ heap_limit ≤ heap_limit_max < 2^H,
atop ≤ 2^A_tab, fp ≤ sp ≤ stack_limit ≤ 2^S_cells, depth ≤ depth_limit ≤
2^Dc − 2, each counter advancing only when its flag is clear), so an
executor MAY use wider counters with the same F_B (§5.7); `W_cell` below
uses the reference Sc.

**Initial allocator state** (oracle entry): `heap_used = 0`, bump =
`heap_base`, `atop` = number of pre-entries, `sp = fp = 0`, `depth = 0`,
`alloctab[0..atop)` = one pinned live entry per global in `layout.
globals` order then per mapped interface region in `layout.interface_
regions` order, storage of `mem` all zero except global images.

**Locations and the result name.** Locations of a function are numbered
in declaration order: an oracle's `regs`; for an lproc instance its
`reg[N]` parameters, then its **result name** (`(r:reg[8])` declares a
location `r` of the lproc, written like any location and read by `lift.ret`), then its
`regs`. Every location of the oracle and its instantiated closure has
a global id (Did bits: index-space order of instances, then declaration
order). `L_f` = all locations of the selected instance of `@f`, m′ =
|L_f| (`reg[N]` params + 1 + regs for an lproc); token, classical and
  separate records. Require exact coverage, no overlap and no gap; mapped
  cells have W=8 as §7.2 requires. Byte `base+j` is exactly wires
  `cells[8*j .. 8*j+8)`. Ordinary `mem` under a mapped interval is never
  selected by an access. Interface shape and origin use K26.
* An activation location list has `id`, `width`, `slot` integers followed
  by `location:wire[width]`, ordered by `id`. The ids and widths are
  exactly §3.7's global ids and declared widths. For a selected instance,
  `slot` is its zero-based declaration index in L_f, including parameters
  and the result name; for a closure delivery list it is the declaration
  index within the location's own instance. A capture list has `position`,
  `width` integers followed by `source:wire[width]`,
  `parameter:wire[width]`, `capture:wire[width]`, ordered by `position`.
  Positions are 0..m-1 in source argument order; parameter and capture
  are the corresponding callee location and frame displacement slot.
  The stack low-width slice for location slot j starts at
  `(old_sp+3+j)*W_cell` in C2 and `(restored_fp+3+j)*W_cell` in R3;
  higher cell bits are untouched. Delivery scans the complete closure
  list in global id order. Reject records inconsistent with those source
  mappings. These lists add no locations for token/classical/region
  arguments; those remain K24/K26 fixed bindings.

* A construction state list has `width`, `initial` integers, `I:image`,
  `storage:allocation`, with empty `order` (array order). Each storage
  identity is distinct, clean and exactly `width` bits; its class is
  `any` for the implicit §5.2 state. In allocation order the list is:
  pc, pc-prime, faulted, code, result_val; then heap_used, atop, sp, fp,
  depth, ordinary mem, stack when layout state exists; locations in global
  id order; local regions in declaration order; alloctab when layout state
  exists; frames 0..B-1. Omit only the layout state excluded by §7.2's
  memoryless-object rule. Counter widths/values are the reference widths
  and initial values of §3.7; all other non-image initial values are zero
  except alloctab pre-entries. [deps: ISA-3.7] A table cell packs base[ptr_width],
  size[ptr_width], live, pinned, in that LSB-first order; pre-entries are
  in §3.7's layout order and unused entries are zero. Image-bearing mem
  and local regions have initial=0. Ordinary mem's I is exactly 2^H
  bytes, initially zero, with each global's image overlaid at its layout
  base; bytes hidden by mapped interface regions remain zero. A local
  region's I is its exact bound data image, or empty for absent/default
  zero. Every other record has I empty and initial
  the nonnegative integer encoding of its initial bit vector, strictly
  below 2^width. Mapped interface regions are borrowed inputs, never
  acquired by this state list. A separate frame list has `index` integer
  and `frame:allocation`, ordered by index; it contains exactly B entries
  indexed 0..B-1, referring to the identical frame handles in the state
  list, each with §3.6's fixed frame width and field order. [deps: ISA-3.6] Reject any
  mismatch of source state, image, width, class, order or identity. The
  construction acquires/initializes this list, invokes the catalog `step`
  once per frame, commits, then reverses frames and initialization before
  releasing the list in reverse order; source row/successor templates
  supply rows, never a replacement dispatcher or construction body. [deps: ISA-5.2-P3, ISA-3.6, ISA-11.1-P5, ISA-11.1-P22]

### 4.3 Polyvariant cloning

A **variant** is a (function, tier, coherence signature) triple, where the coherence signature
is the tuple of coherence states of the function's parameters and of the may-Co objects it
reads (§10, TDL-2) and is empty outside TDL. The H variant is the original function; every
device variant is a clone. **Clones share every global: data is never cloned.** There is one
classical memory (NS §3.2), shared by H, CC, R and the entry state of every L invocation
(§8.5). Calls inside a variant call the variant of the callee with the caller's tier and the
coherence signature at the call. 0.1's `BD-E-DEVICE-ADDRESS` is deleted: a host function may
take the address of any function (audit B-16).

### 4.4 Function addresses

Every function whose address is used has the **function address** A(F): its address in the
driver's **comparison link** (§19.5: non-PIE, fixed section addresses, identical-code folding and
constant merging off; NS §3.2, SQLite §5.2(ii)). A(F) is the value of `ptr @F` in every tier:

- in host code, by linking the production host image with the comparison layout whenever an
  address may cross the host–device boundary (§19.5);
- in CC and R code, as `const` plus `inttoptr` (ISA §4.1);
- in lifted code, as `lift.const` (ISA §4.2);
- in every data image: global initialisers, vtables, function tables (SQLite's `aBuiltinFunc`,
  VFS and pcache method tables).

So `ptrtoint`, comparison, hashing and storage of function pointers equal native execution.
Each tier maps A(F) to its own variant of F by the dispatch of §14.2. Two functions never have
equal A(F) (identical-code folding is off), so the map is well defined. A `blockaddress`
constant is §14.9.

Host and device variants MUST retain a common comparison-link function address and shared global data, while dispatch selects the variant for the caller's tier and coherence signature. [deps: DLT-4.3-P1, DLT-4.4, DLT-19.5, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2, ISA-9.1]

### 4.5 Calls out of the device program

A call in a device variant of a function with no definition resolves, in this order:

1. a marker (§3.2) or a primitive (§3.1);
2. a **runtime shim**: a definition of the symbol in `bennett-runtimes` or `bennett-arith`
### 8.5 Entry state

**Reference semantics.** The region function runs on a **private copy of the caller's whole
classical memory at the call**, with the bound inputs; its writes are discarded except through
`OUT`, `tgt` and the bound device outputs (NS §4.2: "every other write being restored
workspace"). Constant globals, and mutable globals that only the oracle's own L closure writes,
equal their initialisers at every call.

**Realisation on ISA 0.8.** Let R be the abstract objects the oracle closure may load (§5.4),
minus constant globals and mutable globals written by no function outside the L closure.
Translate source read-set objects after normalisation to runtime backing allocations. If
R includes a caller alloca/frame, include its live shadow-stack backing storage and metadata,
bytes and addresses. If reachability is uncertain, include all live caller heap/stack backing
allocations. Whenever the L closure uses the shadow stack, add its state globals to G and
its block to Hb with current base, size and stack pointer, even without a source heap object.
Include the logical frame/allocation table of §17.3, dynamic extents, liveness and
save/restore state in G whenever stack access or stack operations can execute. Replay
these bytes before private allocations/accesses and let ss_init adopt them; a live
backing entry alone does not recreate live program frames. All such metadata mutations
invalidate snapshot reuse independently of the byte read set.
Allocator metadata is an independent entry-state dependency: allocation, deallocation,
allocation-validity checks and observation of allocation-derived addresses read it even
without an LLVM load. Replay the complete caller allocation history (sizes and live bits)
whenever any can execute, including when the byte read set R is empty. Bytes are included
only for byte dependencies; the sound default is the complete table, with a smaller proven
dependency optional. Only when both byte and allocator-state dependencies are absent may
transfer be omitted and globals start at their images
(ISA §3.7), as in rung 4 route A (SQLite §5.2(i)). After replay heap_used, atop and stack-state
values equal the caller's call-entry values.
Otherwise the plugin emits an **entry-state transfer**:

1. **Snapshot.** At the call the caller's tier forms the byte string
   S = k ‖ (size_i ‖ live_i)_{i<k} ‖ G ‖ Hb, where k is an 8-byte little-endian count of heap allocations in the
   caller's allocation table (in allocation order, ISA §7.1), `size_i` and `live_i` are 8-byte
   little-endian fields (`live_i` is 0 or 1), G is the bytes of the mutable globals in R in `layout.globals` order,
   and Hb is the bytes of every live heap allocation in allocation order if R contains a heap
   object or shadow-stack backing allocation (else empty); each live Hb segment has exactly size_i bytes, even for size_i=0. S is zero-padded to 2^A_s bytes, where A_s is the smallest exponent with
   2^A_s ≥ 8 + 16·N_alloc + Σ|G| + `heap_limit_max` (N_alloc the allocation-table capacity of
   the layout, §19.5); A_s is static.
   The snapshot bytes S are assembled in a plugin-added unnamed CC global of 2^A_s bytes,
   zero-filled before every build; thus all 16-byte leaf loads, including padding, are valid.
2. **Register.** The CC tier builds a register of 8·2^A_s wires holding S with a **concat
   tree** of A_s − 3 static-width CC functions `B_j` (j = 4, …, A_s): `B_4(p)` executes
   `qreg.new any 128`, loads the 16 bytes at p as two `i64` values and makes the `oracle_call`
   of the runtime oracle `@xor128` (§16.5) that XORs them into the register (with a token minted
   at launch for budget (6, 0, 0, 0) and a fresh 4-wire fault register, which stays clean and is
   freed); `B_j(p)` (j > 4)
   returns `qreg.concat B_{j−1}(p) B_{j−1}(p + 2^{j−1})`. Every type is static and the cost is
   2^(A_s−4) region calls. The register is bound as an extra `in` region `s0:region[A_s,8]`.
3. **Replay.** The wrapper prologue (§16.2) calls the runtimes function
   `__bennett_rt_entry_replay(s0)`, which for i < k executes `__bennett_nz_alloc(size_i)` (ISA
   `lift.alloc`) and, if `live_i` = 0, `__bennett_nz_free` of the result. ISA §4.2 `lift.alloc`
   and ISA §7.1 `alloc` share the rule "bump + max(n, 1) from `heap_base`", so the replay
   reproduces the caller's heap addresses exactly (audit O-07). It then copies G into the
   globals (by the driver-generated table `__bennett_rt_entry_table` of their addresses and
   sizes) and Hb into the replayed live allocations.
4. **Release.** The snapshot register holds the basis state |S⟩ and is unentangled after every
   oracle call (an `in` region is unchanged on every basis configuration, ISA §5.2). The caller
   keeps it for later calls only while its dependent bytes and allocator metadata are unchanged. Relevant alloc/free
   and shadow-stack push/pop/restore invalidate it as well as stores to R,
   and releases it by unpreparation: retain the classical S for the register's lifetime,
   take successive slice views of at most 128 bits, invoke the same @xor128 preparation
   oracle with the original two i64 arguments on each 128-bit chunk (one view per call),
   then qreg.free the now-clean whole register. A_s ≥ 4, so every leaf is exactly 128 bits.
   No whole-register measurement, view measurement or un-concat operation is needed.

The replay's rows, allocations and calls are part of the reference body and count against the
### 19.4 Placement: host vs device CC

H functions stay native unless they share **pointer-rich** memory with device code: a pointer
argument of a device-entry call, or a global accessed by both, whose pointee may contain pointers
that device code reads (§5). Such H functions are **promoted** to CC variants, transitively up to
their roots (in the limit `main`, and then H is the system-call proxy only: `hostcall
bennett.syscall`). Results are equal by the materialised semantics; ISA-11 would let them stay
native (an efficiency and placement item, §25.3; outline E11).

### 19.5 Layout: the comparison link

The driver links the materialised module natively (§17.7) with a linker script that fixes the
addresses: non-PIE; identical-code folding and constant merging off; the null region [0,
`null_size`) with `null_size` = 4096; the data sections (`.rodata`, `.data`, `.bss`, in that order)
from `data_base` = 4096; the text from `text_base` = 0x400000, or, when the device ranges below
(data, heap, arena, interface regions, computed first) end above 0x400000, from the first multiple
of 0x200000 at or after their end, so that the native comparison binary maps its code disjointly
from them; the defaults are options (`--bennett-layout-data-base`, `--bennett-layout-text-base`,
§24). The link map gives A(F) for
every function and the base of every global. The device `layout` (ISA §9.1) replicates it:

- `globals`: every global of the link at its link address with its image;
- `heap_base`: the first multiple of 4096 at or after the end of the data; `heap_limit_max`: the
  largest `heap` of the budget table (which covers the shadow stack, §9.2);
- the arena (TDL-4) at the first multiple of its size 2^A at or after the heap's end, with 2^A the
  smallest power of two covering the may-Co globals and the coherent allocator's heap (its size
  from `--bennett-arena-heap`, default the largest `heap` of the table); the arena's shadow
  `__bennett_rt_shadow` is a global of the data sections;
- interface regions per oracle above the arena (§16.3); H, A_tab and S_cells by §16.3;
- the production host image is linked with the same addresses for every function and global
  whose address may cross the boundary (§4.4).

*(non-normative)* With data low and text high, H covers the data, heap and regions; function
addresses are values only and never occupy device memory.

**Native layout ABI v1.** The driver MUST define exactly one external constant `@__bennett_rt_native_layout = constant { i64, i64, i64 } { i64 1, i64 heap_base, i64 heap_limit_max }, align 8` in comparison-only support, with fields at byte offsets 0, 8 and 16 in target little-endian order and values from this same resolved §19.5 layout; runtimes declares that object and validates the version/range before use, with no weak/default definition. The third field is a byte capacity, not an end address; it includes shadow-stack allocations under §9.2. This object has no relationship to blockaddress identity objects `__bennett_rt_blk.<f>`.

### 19.6 Build-time snapshots

`--bennett-snapshot=<dump>` rewrites the initialisers of the mutable globals listed in the dump
(and the platform RAM global, §20.1) to the dump's bytes before the plugin runs; the data images
then carry the snapshot and R of §8.5 is empty for them (rung 7: "kernel state snapshot fixed
classically after boot"). The dump is recorded by hash in the build record.

### 19.7 Closed-unit mode

`-bennett-closed-unit` (the REPL fast path, NS §3.3: "a per-TU invocation remains available as a
fast path for programs closed within one unit, with the same semantics"): the plugin runs on one
module plus the runtimes and arith bitcode, after a **closedness check**: every device function's
callees are defined in that set or are trapped exactly as in whole-program mode; no device
function's address escapes the module; no function pointer enters through a device-entry
parameter. If the check passes, processing is identical to whole-program mode. If it fails, the
driver falls back to whole-program mode (it does not reject). Objects are content-addressed (ISA
§9.2) and linked at launch (ISA §9.3), so the REPL is incremental.

Closed-unit mode MUST retain whole-program semantics after its closedness check and fall back to whole-program processing when that check fails. [deps: DLT-19.1, DLT-19.7, ISA-9.2, ISA-9.3]

### 19.8 Mixed LLVM majors

The driver reads every bitcode input with the newest major M of the envelope (bitcode is readable
by newer majors), links with M and runs plugin M; runtimes and arith ship bitcode for each M.
Every input MUST have one target datalayout and triple (NS §3.2) (`BD-E-VERSION` otherwise: a
tool-version mismatch, not a program property). An input newer than M is `BD-E-VERSION`. The
tested (front end, major) pairs are published in the envelope; a corpus case links clang 18
bitcode with Julia's and rustc's majors (§21.2; bd `.16`).

Mixed-major bitcode links within the tested envelope MUST use its newest reader and matching plugin and library bitcode, retaining one target triple and DataLayout. [deps: DLT-19.8, DLT-21.1, ISA-9.1, ISA-9.2]

"""Generate the complete draft catalog or historical family checkpoints. Python -I, stdlib only.
No interpreter imports. Does not publish or update the normative hash marker.
"""
import argparse
import hashlib
import json
import runpy
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
def jcs(v):
    # This catalog uses integer numbers below 2**53; no floats. UTF-16 key order.
    if type(v) is bool: return "true" if v else "false"
    if isinstance(v, dict):
        return '{' + ','.join(jcs(k)+':'+jcs(v[k]) for k in sorted(v, key=lambda k:k.encode('utf-16be'))) + '}'
    if isinstance(v, list):
        return '[' + ','.join(map(jcs,v)) + ']'
    if isinstance(v, str):
        return json.dumps(v, ensure_ascii=False, separators=(',', ':'))
    if type(v) is int and abs(v) < 2**53:
        return str(v)
    raise ValueError(f'outside catalog JCS subset: {v!r}')
def plus(a,b): return ['+',a,b]
def minus(a,b): return ['-',a,b]
def times(a,b): return ['*',a,b]
def wire(name, bit=0): return name+'['+jcs(bit)+']'
def gate(g,*w): return {'gate':g,'wires':list(w)}
def alloc(n,w): return {'alloc':n,'width':w,'class':'any','state':'clean'}
def release(n): return {'release':n}
def loop(i,n,b): return {'for':i,'from':0,'to':n,'body':b}
def inv(b): return {'inverse':True,'body':b}
class Circuit:
    def __init__(self): self.body=[]; self.scratch=[]; self.serial=0
    def temp(self,width=1):
        n=f'z{self.serial}'; self.serial+=1
        self.scratch.append(n); self.body.append(alloc(n,width)); return n
    def bit(self): return wire(self.temp())
    def boolean(self,e,out):
        """Postorder tree, fresh scratch per node, no CSE; root XOR out."""
        computes=[]; names=[]
        def visit(e):
            if isinstance(e,str): return e
            op,*args=e
            if op=='or': return visit(('xor',('xor',*args),('and',*args)))
            ws=[visit(a) for a in args]
            n=self.temp(); names.append(n); d=wire(n)
            if op=='not': ops=[gate('x',d),gate('cx',ws[0],d)]
            elif op=='xor': ops=[gate('cx',s,d) for s in ws]
            elif op=='and': ops=[gate('cx',ws[0],d)] if ws[0]==ws[1] else [gate('ccx',*ws,d)]
            else: raise ValueError(op)
            self.body.extend(ops); computes.extend(ops); return d
        root=visit(e); self.body.append(gate('cx',root,out)); self.body.append(inv(computes))
        self.body.extend(release(n) for n in reversed(names))
    def ripple(self,a,b,n,sub=False):
        p=self.temp(n); s=self.temp(n); c=self.temp(plus(n,1))
        body_start=len(self.body)
        if sub: self.body.append(gate('x',wire(c)))
        start=len(self.body)
        aa=a('i'); bb=b('i')
        self.boolean(('xor',aa,('not',bb) if sub else bb),wire(p,'i'))
        self.boolean(('xor',wire(p,'i'),wire(c,'i')),wire(s,'i'))
        self.boolean(('xor',('and',aa,('not',bb) if sub else bb),('and',wire(p,'i'),wire(c,'i'))),wire(c,plus('i',1)))
        bitbody=self.body[start:]; self.body[start:]=[loop('i',n,bitbody)]
        return s,c,body_start
    def finish(self,outops,start=0):
        compute=self.body[start:]; self.body=self.body[:start]+compute+outops+[inv(compute)]
    def recipe(self,kind,params,source):
        return {'kind':kind,'parameters':params,'body':self.body,'scratch':self.scratch,'removable':[], 'source':source}
def recipe(kind,params,body,source,scratch=()):
    return {'kind':kind,'parameters':params,'body':body,'scratch':list(scratch),'removable':[],'source':source}
def copy_family():
    out=[]
    for k in ['qxor','qcopy','quncopy','qexch']:
        op=gate('swap',wire('a','i'),wire('b','i')) if k=='qexch' else gate('cx',wire('s','i'),wire('t','i'))
        body=[loop('i','N',[op])]
        if k=='qcopy': body.insert(0,alloc('t','N'))
        if k=='quncopy': body += [inv([alloc('t','N')])]
        out.append(recipe(k,['N'],body,'0.8 §4.5: '+('swap a[i] b[i]' if k=='qexch' else 'cx s[i] t[i]')))
    for k,ws in [('x',['a']),('cx',['a','b']),('ccx',['a','b','t']),('swap',['a','b'])]:
        out.append(recipe('gate.'+k,[],[gate(k,*map(wire,ws))],'0.8 §4.4: '+k+' is exact, including global phase.'))
    return out
def arithmetic_family():
    out=[]
    for op in ['add','sub']:
        c=Circuit(); s,carry,_=c.ripple(lambda i:wire('a',i),lambda i:wire('b',i),'N',op=='sub')

    11	## North Star gap
    12	
    13	This is a real missing boundary capability for North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4: native main must call marked modules while preserving ordinary pointer-rich sharing. Promoting main or copying unrelated buffers cannot be the only eventual realization. Existing rungs and device lowering remain valid and independent of this amendment.

    15	## Alternatives and witnesses
    16	
    17	| Construction | Witness and consequence |
    18	|---|---|
    19	| Treat `ptr` as INT | Native address 0x1000 can denote unmapped device storage, a different global, or stale storage after reuse. Width agreement supplies no ownership. Unsound as registration. |
    20	| Snapshot into independent buffers | Two native pointers to one interior cell must observe the same write; two independent copies can disagree. Useful only with an explicit alias-preserving import/export protocol. |
    21	| Promote all pointer-sharing callers | Expresses device computation but converts native main in the cyclic graph case. Preserve as an option; it does not witness the mixed capability. |
    22	| Versioned registration and native adapter | Canonical allocation identities, alias-preserving address observations and leases support native calls and reversal. Recommended. Same-address and imported-private-oracle profiles have distinct evidence. |
    23	
    24	Concrete acceptance witness: native bytes `[10,20,30,40]`; call A adds 3 to byte 1 and closes, yielding `[10,23,30,40]`. Native code sets byte 0 to 99. Call B XORs byte 0 with 1; reversing B **before close** restores `[99,23,30,40]`. A stale first-call snapshot would incorrectly restore 10. A second witness has two cyclic nodes and two arguments to one interior value: write 7 through one alias, read 7 through the other, preserve pointer equality and address-derived outputs. The finite model in [verify.py](verify.py) checks the bookkeeping and alias counterexample; it is not VM execution.

    27	
    28	Append to §10 Model, and cross-reference from §§7.1–7.2:
    29	
    30	```text
    31	Native process entry and device CC entry are separate roles. A mixed
    32	container MAY expose a native callable symbol whose adapter selects a
    33	device program and CC entry. launch 0.9's existing argument kinds remain
    34	by-value; integer bits do not register native storage. Native calls with
    35	ordinary pointers or aggregates use an explicitly negotiated mixed-launch
    36	and memory profile. The following requirements govern that profile.
    37	
    38	The adapter descriptor binds container component/local-entry, linked ISA
    39	object_hash, entry signature and closure_hash, target ABI/triple/DataLayout,
    40	layout/mapping profile, certificate/build record, and runtime/service
    41	versions. It specifies positional scalar and aggregate decomposition,
    42	field offsets, padding treatment (unobservable padding is zero-filled),
    43	endianness, integer widths and exact float bits. Data pointers, function
    44	pointers, opaque launch ids and tokens have distinct descriptor kinds.
    45	Pointers are capabilities plus checked offsets; raw integers grant no
    46	native access. Null and one-past values retain their source semantics;
    47	one-past is not a dereferenceable byte.
    48	
    49	A registration records canonical allocation identity and generation,
    50	owner/liveness authority, extent, permissions, target layout and mapping,
    51	allocator metadata policy, and current entry epoch. Interior/overlapping
    52	views resolve to that same identity. Mapping preserves aliasing and all
    53	defined pointer observations, including comparisons, ptrtoint/address
    54	hashing and indirect dispatch; it MUST NOT silently swizzle unidentified
    55	integer words. A same-address profile requires matching representation and
    56	nonconflicting ranges. Other mappings require a separately published
    57	observation-preserving encoding/relocation protocol, not a capability cap.
    58	
    59	Before access, validate nonwrapping ranges, generation, liveness, layout,
    60	permissions and ownership. Device-private allocations keep §7.1 semantics.
    61	Imported storage supplies equivalent bounds/liveness/allocation metadata;
    62	it does not require native malloc to have executed the device bump history.
    63	Escaping allocations require a host-compatible allocation/transfer profile
    64	covering native free/realloc, aliases, budgets and reversible metadata.
    65	A private device address alone cannot become a returned native pointer.
    66	
    67	For the exclusive mutable profile, acquire a coordinated lease before the
    68	run and retain it through CLOSED. All aliases and programs sharing that
    69	storage participate in the same coordinator. Host/free/realloc/unregister
    70	and conflicting runs cannot access it during the lease; compatible read
    71	leases and disjoint storage may coexist. The liveness authority must
    72	prevent or detect invalidation; hashing entry bytes is not an interference
    73	protocol for arbitrary unsynchronised native writes.
    74	
    75	Each invocation binds program/root/run, arguments, registration generations
    76	and entry epochs. COMPLETED/FAILED retain ownership. Eligible reverse
    77	restores this invocation's entry bytes and allocator/ownership history
    78	before close, retaining §10's measurement/free restrictions. A failed
    79	operation makes no partial change; earlier successful operations remain
    80	in the failed prefix and reverse under §§7.4/10. Close commits the exact
    81	remaining prefix and releases leases. This is synchronised visibility,
    82	not atomic publication of an arbitrary graph. After close a later native
    83	edit is part of the next invocation's entry state.
    84	
    85	The synchronous adapter waits through HOST_REQUEST and suspension under
    86	§10, distinguishes API failure from a FAILED device result/fault, extracts
    87	declared results, closes and returns to the native caller. An asynchronous
    88	adapter returns an opaque run owner; it does not grant early access to
    89	leased memory. No exception crosses the C boundary implicitly. Profile
    90	negotiation, stale/wrong-owner/unregistered pointer, range/layout mismatch,
    91	interference and failed-operation diagnostics are distinct published
    92	status outcomes; assign their wire encodings with the new ABI version.
    93	Do not allocate numeric tags in launch 0.9's existing enum.
    94	
    95	Lifted/oracle memory is a private coherent construction. Import a
    96	consistent entry image and equivalent metadata before private allocation,
    97	then retain the prescribed forward/reverse semantics. A quantum address
    98	does not cause a native RAM read. Current copied launches and snapshot/
    99	replay remain admitted constructions, not the sole eventual native-sharing
   100	capability. Device effects, CC replies and native I/O retain separate owners.
   101	```
   102	
   103	Publish the descriptor and C wire layouts as one versioned companion contract before implementing it in driver/runtime/VM. The text deliberately leaves numeric encodings for that publication, rather than presenting a partial C header as ready to consume. F23 should consume this publication and the F15/F16 mapping rules; retain the VM's existing integer-address backend and by-value ABI meanwhile.
   104	
   105	## Hashes, catalog and consumers
   106	
   107	Runtime registrations/addresses belong to invocation records, never immutable ISA source identities. Container bytes/digest, payload bytes/digest, `object_hash`, root closure, certificate and runtime profile are separate bindings. Changed imported images/layouts or generated wrappers can change actual object/closure/schedule/certificate hashes. A memory profile alone does not change `ref@0.9` recipe bytes. Driver, runtimes, VM, plugin and conformance must pin the accepted contract independently; no consumer imports VM source as the protocol.
   108	
   109	## Conformance vectors and actual verification
   110	
   111	Require scalar/F64/QREG/TOKEN compatibility; aggregate padding and float-bit round trips; both concrete witnesses above; interior/overlapping/cyclic aliases; host edits between closed runs; free/reuse at the same address; null/one-past/range overflow; stale/wrong-program generations; conflicting leases; failed-store prefix and reverse; measurement ineligibility; returned host-owned allocations and native realloc; function pointers; two embedded roots; lifted private import and metadata-only invalidation. Compare the same materialised device closure and layout, preserving original pointer identity for address-sensitive baselines. Unregistered numeric CLI pointers and `--mem` hex copies are negative evidence controls. These are proposed integration vectors, not claims of an implemented backend. [INDEX](INDEX.md) records the actual bounded checks.
   112	
   113	## K entry: 0.9 versus 0.10
   114	
   115	Recommend a versioned companion `mixed-launch/memory` contract, normatively referenced from ISA 0.10 and the next dialect revision. A 0.9 K entry may clarify the existing by-value boundary, but cannot add pointer tags or memory semantics silently. Any in-version K route needs **council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules**, including negotiation and status encodings. Assign numeric K ids only on acceptance. No recipe catalog change is required by this design alone.
   116	
   117	## Mixed fat object
   118	
   119	Apply [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F04/F08/F23. Native main, argv/envp and host-only libc remain native; only marked modules and their complete device closures enter ISA inventory. Each root keeps its own certificate/context `[]`, budget/ledger and run/tape association. Shared storage uses explicit coordination across those roots. This keeps the mixed fat object reachable; it neither implements it nor makes its amendments prerequisites for rungs 3/4. F30 stays deferred.

### 11.1 Reference-library contract (K11)

**ISA-REF-LIB** (K11; b-isa-spec-8r4, from bennett-vm-47t).
This is the decided 0.9 contract. The catalog bound as ref version 0.9
is the file `spec/ref-0.9.json` whose four-line envelope hashes to
`5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197`.
It publishes the complete 219-recipe catalog, byte-identical to the
accepted catalog snapshot. Consumers lacking the bound library report
`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` as §11 requires. The witness below is not a
full-library hash.

**Serialization and identity.** The schema and family witnesses are
documented in `spec/ref-0.9-draft.README.md`. The JSON file is RFC 8785 canonical
UTF-8, with no trailing LF, containing exactly `isa`, `name`, `version`
and `recipes`. The first three are strings `0.9`, `ref`, `0.9`.
`recipes` is an array in bytewise `kind` order, then increasing parameter
tuple order. Each instantiated recipe has `kind`, its integer width or
other named integer parameters, `operations`, `scratch`, `removable`
and `source` (the motivating ISA sentence). A parameterised recipe
uses `parameters` (an ordered array of formals defined below) and `body` instead of the
instance's `operations`; its body uses the closed constructors below.
Recipes cover every §4 instruction/row, the §3.6 step, §3.7 activation
operations and §5.2 construction, with no implementation-private leaf.
Classical instructions/effects remain classical leaf events (§8.2),
not invented quantum gates. The README documents schema, parameters,
coverage and witnesses; it is not hashed and cannot alter operations.

The library hash is SHA-256 of this UTF-8 envelope (every line ends LF,
including the last); the fourth line is the **entire** canonical library
JSON, including all recipes, sources and versions, without an embedded
hash field:

```
isa=0.9
expansion=ref
version=0.9
<JCS library document>
```

`bindings.expansion` names `ref`, version `0.9`, and this 64-lowercase-hex
hash. This adopts §9.2's version-prefixed, LF-terminated **envelope
convention**, with explicit expansion-domain and library-version lines;
it is a new library rule, not §9.2's symbol signature/SCC envelope.
Motivation (0.8 §11): "a versioned document, identified in the schedule's
`bindings.expansion` as { name, version, hash }"; 0.8 §9.2: "Semantic
envelope ... every part ends with LF". A changed recipe, source, scratch
name or ordering changes the library hash; no schedule/certificate can
silently inherit that change (§12.1's realised-subject binding).

**Expansion and ordinals.** Recipe bodies are ordered arrays
of constructors: a gate (`gate`, ordered `wires`, optional `params`), an
allocation (`alloc`, `width`, `class`, `state`), release (`release`),
classical event (`classical`, `point`), counted loop (`for`, `from`,
`to`, `body`), named recipe expansion (`invoke`, ordered `args`), or
inverse (`inverse`, `body`). Constructor objects have exactly those
keys. Fully instantiated `operations` contain only emitted leaves, each
also carrying its computed `ordinal`; unexpanded `body` constructors
carry no ordinal. Scalars are integer literals, parameter names, or prefix arrays
`[op, operands…]` for `+ - * div mod bit`; div is floor division with a
positive divisor. `bit(x,i)` requires nonnegative integers x and i and
returns floor(x/2^i) mod 2, with no fixed machine-word truncation (K23).
For x=13, bit(x,0..4) is 1,0,1,1,0; literal construction emits X only
for the one bits, in increasing bit order. Wires are declaration names with integer-expression
subscripts. Loops enumerate [from,to) in increasing order; expansion is
depth first, array order, and recipe dependencies are acyclic. An
inverse reverses its fully expanded body and adjoints each leaf by
§5.3, interchanging allocation/release. The complete serialized catalog
must encode the algorithms below in these constructors before release;
this document does not claim that prose alone is an interchangeable file.

**Typed binding (K19).** A formal is `{ "name": name, "type": type }`,
with exactly these keys, except `wire`, which additionally requires
`width`, and `templates`, which additionally requires `length` (integer
expressions using earlier integer formals); `arguments` requires `symbol`,
and `value` requires `isa_type`, as specified in K24; K25–K27 add
`records`, `region`, `class`, and `allocation` with the keys specified there. Types are `integer`,
`wire`, `angle`, `body`, `symbol`, `environment`, `event`, `image`,
`templates`, `arguments`, `value`, `records`, `region`, `class`, and
`allocation`. A bare name abbreviates an
`integer` formal, retaining the printed witness bytes. Names are unique
within a parameter list. Width expressions use only earlier integer
formals. A wire formal of width n is the ordered list w[n], with
little-endian indexing, including the empty list when n = 0.

`invoke` binds `args` positionally, evaluating actuals in the caller's
lexical environment before binding any callee formal. Integer actuals use
the scalar grammar above. A wire actual is a wire name, a subscripted
wire, an array of wire actuals concatenated left to right, or exactly
`{ "range": name, "from": scalar, "to": scalar }`, selecting [from,to)
from a named bundle; bounds must satisfy 0 ≤ from ≤ to ≤ its width.
An actual's resulting length must equal its formal's evaluated width.
| Row, oracle and structure | Compose these recipes in the printed §3.6 step order, §4.2 slot order, §3.7 activation order, §5.2 allocate/forward/commit/reverse/release order. `qif`, loops, local-region image X gates and `qinplace` use the literal sequences of §4.5–§4.6, incorporating 0.9's explicit parameters. Cleanup `reverse` adjoints that sequence; `recompute` follows §6.4. | §11: "Above gate level the order is fixed by §3.6, §4.2, §4.5–§4.6 and §5.2"; §5.2: "run the inverse step for t = B..1, then release (1)". |

The permutation algorithms define exact permutation circuits, preserving
every input required by the row and cleaning scratch; circuit correctness must still be checked
against full-domain T3 witnesses and §5.7, not inferred from costs or
names. Complete catalog: **219 recipes**; envelope SHA-256
`5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197`. [deps: ISA-11.1-P2, ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P28, ISA-11.1-P29]
Publication follows acceptance. [deps: ISA-11.1-P1, ISA-11.1-P2] In particular this is
not permission to publish a partial library that omits division, stack,
coherent control, memory or any instruction.

```json
{"isa":"0.9","name":"ref","recipes":[{"kind":"qxor","operations":[{"gate":"cx","ordinal":0,"wires":["s[0]","t[0]"]},{"gate":"cx","ordinal":1,"wires":["s[1]","t[1]"]}],"removable":[],"scratch":[],"source":"0.8 §4.5","width":2}],"version":"0.9"}

## 17. Decisions deferred to implementation, with defaults

Each item is one an implementer must decide to build; any decision is
conformant provided two implementations agree on the default below until
the item is revised. Items 1–5 are asserted non-semantic.

1. **Binary encoding.** Default: none; §16 text is the only interchange
   form.
2. **Gate-name registry.** Default: the names of §4.4 only; an unknown
   gate name is a load error.
3. **Further device models.** Default: the four of §7.3; an `effect` on
   any other device is `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED`.
4. **Mechanisation of §14.** Default: none; the reference interpreter is
   the oracle.
5. **Memory layout of the opaque C objects.** Default: opaque; only the
   listed functions touch them.
6. **Measurement-based cleanup** (§6.4): the normative fixup, its
   controlled variant and its certificate kind. Default: unitary cleanup
   (`reverse`); `capabilities.mbu` is `false`.
7. **Expansion library** (§11): the gate-level expansion below row
   level. Default: the library `ref` published by `bennett-isa-spec`
   (ripple-carry adders and comparators, linear `inrange` scans,
   one-hot address dispatch, clean scratch released in reverse order);
   a schedule that names no `expansion` binds `ref` at the ISA version
   in the object's header, with the full-library envelope hash published
   for that version in §11.1. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4]
   For a 0.9 object this is exactly
   `{"name":"ref","version":"0.9","hash":"5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197"}`,
   equally for omission and an explicit binding. [deps: ISA-11.1-P3, ISA-11.1-P4, ISA-11.1]
