### 16.2 Grammar

```
  object    := header NL section*
  header    := "bennett-object" SP "isa=" VER SP "object=" VER SP "launch=" VER SP "sched=" VER SP "cert=" VER SP "coverage=" VER SP "target=" VER SP "ptr_width=" INT
  section   := NL "section" SP SECTNAME NL body
  SECTNAME  := "symbols" | "imports" | "functions" | "data" | "devices" | "layout" | "schedule" | "certificate" | "coverage"
  body      := jsonline | dataline* | function*
  jsonline  := JCS-canonical JSON object NL
  dataline  := NAME SP HEX NL
  function  := fhead NL regs? block+
  fhead     := ("fn" SP ("cc"|"r") | "oracle" | "action" | "perm_action" | "lproc") SP "@" NAME SP "(" plist ")" SP "(" plist ")" SP "(" plist ")" SP "(" plist ")" SP "(" plist ")"
               -- oracle: (in..) (tgt) (fault) (mem..) (args..) ; action: (mut..) (in..) (mem..) (args..) () ; lproc: (params..) (result) (tokens..) (classical..) (mem..) ; fn: (params..) (results..) () () ()
  plist     := (param ("," SP param)*)?
  param     := NAME ":" type (SP ("in"|"mut"|"token"|"classical"))?
  type      := "i" INT | "ptr" | "f64" | "token" | "wire" | "wire[" INT "]" | "reg[" INT "]" | "region[" INT "," INT "]" | "qreg[" (INT | "*") "]"
  regs      := "regs" SP "{" SP (NAME ":" type ("," SP NAME ":" type)*)? SP "}" (SP "bounds" SP "{" INT ("," SP INT)* "}")? NL
  block     := "^" NAME "(" plist "):" NL instr+
  instr     := (lhs SP "=" SP)? OPCODE (SP operand)* (SP "{" NL instr+ "}" (SP "{" NL instr+ "}")?)? NL
  lhs       := NAME | "(" NAME ("," SP NAME)* ")"
  operand   := NAME | NAME "[" INT "]" | "@" NAME | "^" NAME ("(" (NAME ("," SP NAME)*)? ")")? | INT | FLOAT | STRING | "(" (NAME ("," SP NAME)*)? ")"
  OPCODE    := a mnemonic of §4 with its dotted suffix (§4.8 fixes operand count, kinds and result placement)
  VER       := INT "." INT
```

The empty list operand `()` also spells the address-only empty actual of
§4.6, solely in the address position of its four coherent memory rows
when A = 0. It is not an ordinary wire value.

The oracle header and signature declare no invocation-token slot. Its
catalog interface follows the five printed groups exactly; the separate
`tk` operand binds the typed `oracle_invocation` channel of §11.1, not a
reserved header name or an extra `args` element.

Lproc groups have the types and roles of §3.7. A `token` suffix is
permitted only in the third group and a `classical` suffix only in the
fourth, each agreeing with that group's role; either suffix may be
omitted. The first and second groups carry no suffix, and the fifth
requires `in`. The `token` and `classical` suffixes are not permitted on
other kinds of function or on block parameters or `regs` entries. The additional
`lift.call` lists use the existing list operand grammar (§3.7).

# adg — loaded-program memory lifetime across launches

Proposal for council review; `b-isa-spec-adg`.

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §7.1 defines initial memory, allocation table and bump pointer
without saying whether initialization occurs at load or every run.
§3.2 places the register ledger at program scope; §10 gives a run
exclusive ownership only until CLOSED; §7.4 and T4 restore a run's entry
state on eligible reversal. These strongly favor program-lifetime state
but do not explicitly settle classical memory initialization.

Dialect 0.3 draft §§6.5–6.6 uses a persistent qreg table in device memory
and requires parameter/result roots to survive entry calls. North Star
§3.1's register handles and §5's REPL/library device model require
cross-entry quantum state. A fresh-every-run interpretation loses the
table while live registers remain. This is a contract ambiguity with
observable results, S0 under C1/C5; persistence is the compatible reading.
It adds no restriction to the North Star, including repeated SQLite or
kernel invocations.

## Alternatives and hand-worked witness

Layout: ptr_width=16, H=16, A_tab=2, S_cells=0, null_size=1,
heap_base=16, heap_limit_max=16; a one-byte global g at base 1 with image
00. Run A allocates one byte (returns 16) and stores 7 into g, then
completes and is CLOSED without reversal. Run B loads g and allocates
one byte. Under persistence it returns `(7,17)`; under reinitialization
it returns `(0,16)`. These are distinct visible results from the same
loaded program. Freeing 16 does not reclaim storage under §7.1.

If A is instead successfully REVERSED before CLOSED, its allocation
and store are undone, and B returns `(0,16)`. Merely closing A does not
reverse it. An eligible failed run retains its executed prefix until it
is reversed; closing a failed run does not silently roll it back.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Program-lifetime memory and ledger (recommended) | Matches §10 ownership and the dialect's persistent table. |
| Reinitialize on each run | Would need a different host-held table and an explicit reset/lifetime ABI; it changes existing consumer assumptions. |
| Implicit reset when no register is live | Still changes classical allocation/global results; no clause authorizes it. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Insert after §7.1's Initial state sentence:

```diff
+That state is established once when the program is loaded. Classical
+memory, allocation-table entries and the bump pointer belong to the
+loaded program and persist across runs. For sequential launches, a new
+run starts from the state left by the preceding CLOSED run. Closing a run does not restore its
+entry state. If the preceding run was successfully REVERSED before
+being CLOSED, its reversible state is its restored entry state (§7.4,
+§10, T4). A FAILED run otherwise leaves its executed prefix in place.
+Register-ledger entries and live register identities likewise persist
+under §3.3; ownership ends at CLOSED, not the register lifetime. Unloading
+and loading a program creates a new program instance with initial state;
+register ids of the old instance are not imported into it.
```

Append to §10's ownership paragraph:

```diff
+Persistence does not relax exclusive ownership of touched registers or
+memory while a run is not CLOSED. Separate loaded program instances have
+separate state; initialization is never repeated merely to start a run.
```

All examples here use sequential CLOSED runs. This wording does not add a
blanket prohibition on disjoint concurrent runs or invent a memory merge
rule. The existing ownership restrictions still govern overlapping uses.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

This pins an existing reading: no source, envelope, target or catalog byte
changes and no hash re-pin. A consumer that reset memory on each run must
change its load/run lifecycle; plugin qreg-table code should not change.
Launch bindings and certificates that replay a later entry must include
its actual entry-state/preparation assumptions rather than presume a
fresh load. Domain claims do not acquire coverage of all histories merely
by showing one initialized run. Header diagnostics already include
E_INVALID_STATE; no new numeric status is proposed.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Test load→A→CLOSED→B and load→A→REVERSED→CLOSED→B with the results
above; check every byte, table live bit, bump pointer, register identity
and token lifetime. Add a failed A whose prefix stored 7: B sees 7 unless
that prefix was reversed. If B passes a register still owned by A before
A is CLOSED, it must return E_INVALID_STATE before effects. Fresh loading
restores g=0 and bump=16. A stored id can be recovered by qreg.from_id in
the next run after ownership is released. These are hand derivations;
the scalar state transitions are checked by
[contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md), not by a
new reference execution.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend a **0.9 K wording repair** to §§7.1/10 with lifecycle vectors.
# F11 — define the complete device-state closure

Bead: **b-dialect-spec-hsb**. Amendment proposal, never a prerequisite for rungs 3/4; F30 remains deferred until rung 3 or 4 lands.

## Exact defect and clause citations

[Dialect 0.2.12](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md) §19.5 says `globals: every global of the link`. §§4.3/17.1 require one semantic memory/shared globals. The [0.3 draft](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md) §§4.3, 17.1–17.2, 19.5 likewise speaks of program globals and the complete link, while adding qtab/arena-specific ordering. Neither distinguishes host-only native globals from a selected device program's state inventory. A literal whole-native-link inventory drags native TLS/libc/unrelated state into ISA layout/certificates. See [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F11.

## North Star gap

Yes if the universal inventory is applied to mixed mode (North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). Device closure completeness remains mandatory. Native-only state can stay outside ISA memory only after a sound closure proof; merely naming a global “host” does not prove it unreachable from a device load or indirect call.

## Alternatives and witnesses

Native main uses native_tls and native_counter; marked root reaches shared.value through table[fp], and bounds checking also reads alloc_meta. A direct-symbol-only scan can miss shared.value or alloc_meta. Including the entire native link imports native_tls needlessly. Cloning shared.value for H and device gives two values after a native write. Prefer a conservative state/dependency fixed point with an explicit boundary binding. The finite closure witness in [verify.py](verify.py) requires shared.value, table, fp, alloc_meta and transitive callee state, while excluding native_tls/native_counter.

## Recommended exact dialect diff

Insert before §19.5's layout construction in both future tracks:

```text
A complete device link is the selected device roots, all device variants
reachable through direct calls, region candidates and every conservative
indirect candidate, their required runtime/arith bodies, and the complete
state dependency closure below. Whole-program analysis may inspect more
native code; inspection does not put that code or state into this link.

Seed the state closure with globals, initialisers and boundary-visible
shared allocations that any selected variant may read/write/address or
observe through a defined pointer operation. Close it under reachable
initialiser references, aliases, pointer-containing fields, function-pointer
targets and their variant dependencies, allocation bounds/liveness,
allocator, shadow-stack and other metadata affecting execution or faults.
Include all runtime-generated state required by the selected construction.
Unknown may-alias/indirect targets require a sound conservative dependency
set; uncertainty is not permission to drop state or reject the source.

Native-only functions, globals, TLS and libc state outside this closure
need no ISA layout/certificate record. Shared state has one semantic
identity across H and device variants; it is neither independently cloned
nor omitted. Its concrete storage and entry epoch are supplied by the
selected mixed memory/layout profile. “Native-only” is a proved absence
from the dependency closure, not a producer label overriding reachability.
```

In 0.2 §19.5 replace `globals: every global of the link at its link address with its image` with `globals: every device-state global in the complete device link, at its profile-bound address with its entry image; native-only globals remain outside this inventory`. In 0.3 apply the same scope to “every program global” and ordinary data placement, **retaining** its single range-order rule, qtab exclusion/placement, may-Co relocation, per-oracle interfaces and all nonoverlap/capacity checks. Device qtab/shadow/allocator state required by that construction remains in scope even without a direct user load.

Append to §§4.3/17.1:

```text
One classical memory means one semantic identity for genuinely shared
state. It does not require native-only state in every embedded ISA object.
Each selected device program imports/binds its complete state closure under
§19.5 and the negotiated memory profile. All device-reachable helper and
fault-metadata dependencies remain mandatory.
```

For uncertain dependencies, the sound analysis may conservatively include state while exact mapping/publication work remains an implementation obligation. It must not turn an incomplete analysis into permission to force every host function into device code.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Removing unrelated state changes actual layout/images and often H/A_tab, object/closure/schedule/certificate hashes and frozen capacities. Rebuild the native comparator and device output from the same selected closure; bind the new layout/profile in the container. The recipe catalog is unchanged unless its source changes independently. Producers/checkers need reproducible dependency evidence; no consumer may trust a host-only label without validating the closure against the selected module and build inputs.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Test direct/shared globals, cyclic initialisers, indirect targets, aliases, metadata-only dependency, native TLS/libc state, 0.3 qtab/arena state and two roots sharing a global under coordinated leases. Omit each required dependency in turn and require a missing binding/closure diagnostic. Mutating the shared global natively between closed calls must change the next entry image; independent clones must fail the alias observation. [INDEX](INDEX.md) reports finite graph/citation checks only, not a complete points-to analysis or backend test.

## K entry: 0.9 versus 0.10

This is a dialect state-scope amendment: next versioned maintained revision plus 0.3 draft, no automatic ISA K. ISA 0.9 layout validation remains intact. Any semantic memory extension is the companion/0.10 route in [native-sharing](native-sharing.md). An in-version ISA K route requires **council + maintainer authorization of compatibility rules**. Suggested dialect decision: `DEVICE-STATE-CLOSURE`, with the exact dependency rule above; no silent change to 0.2.12.

## Mixed fat object

Apply [fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) and [AUDIT.md](../2026-11-02-4ht-audit/AUDIT.md) F11. Native main/argv/envp/libc remain outside device inventory unless their state is genuinely shared and explicitly bound. Marked roots have separate context `[]`, certificates, budget/ledger and tape associations, with complete shared device state. This addresses the inventory lock-out without shrinking any device obligation. These amendments never gate rungs 3/4; F30 remains deferred.

yields `iN`; signed division truncates toward zero. The
literal c of `const.iN c` and `lift.const.iN c` satisfies −2^(N−1) ≤ c <
2^N and denotes c mod 2^N (both the signed and the unsigned spelling of
every value are accepted); any other literal is ill-formed (suite F).

**Lifted width rules** (suite F; a mismatch is ill-formed, never a
truncation or an extension). Every address operand (`a` of `lift.load`/
`lift.store`, `p` of `lift.boundscheck`/`lift.free`) and every address
result (`w` of `lift.addr`/`lift.alloc`) is `reg[ptr_width]`; the length
`n` of `lift.alloc`/`lift.boundscheck` is a location of any width or a
literal in [0, 2^ptr_width); `inrange` and every prospective sum are
computed at width max(widths of the operands involved) + 1 with no wrap.
The destination of `lift.label` and the operand of `qjumpr` have any
width N with 2^N > n (the index-space size). `lift.call` location
arguments have exactly the callee's parameter widths and its destination the callee's
result width; its token, classical and region lists match the callee's
corresponding groups in count, order and type (§3.7); `lift.ret v` has
the containing lproc's result width;
`lift.oracle_call`/`lift.oracle_uncall`/`lift.perm_call` operands have
exactly the callee signature widths, and `mem` operands name caller
regions whose `[A,W]` equal the callee's and whose role is `in` for an
`in` slot (an `in` region is never passed to a `mut` slot); `fault C` and
`lift.faultif p C` require the literal C < 2^F, where F is the fault
width of the containing oracle or, in an lproc, of every oracle whose
lproc closure contains it (checked at load and again at link; an lproc
in no oracle's closure is checked when linked into one). The equal-width and widening/narrowing constraints above apply to lifted rows on their location and pseudo-location bit-vectors as specified in §4.2; the CC/R iN and ptr operand-type restrictions do not retype lifted locations. The destinations of one `lift.parmov` are pairwise distinct.

**Quantum registers in CC and R (K88).**

A register denotes an ordered vector of n device-resident wires with a
resolved target class for each wire. The register ledger records that
class vector, width, nonzero id and live/viewed/consumed state. A single
class is shorthand for a homogeneous vector. qreg.new cls n and
bennett_qreg_new allocate n wires of cls and remain homogeneous requests.
qreg.concat accepts differing operand classes and concatenates their
wire and class vectors in operand order, with no movement, copy,
measurement or reallocation. Its existing distinct/live/non-view
preconditions and consumption/new-id transitions remain unchanged.
qreg.slice selects the corresponding wire/class subsequences, with its
existing view ownership and one-call consumption rule. A slice never
relabels a wire. Zero-width runtime registers have empty vectors.
Ledger frames retain enough information to restore the complete class
vectors, wire ordering, identities and handle states. In particular free
records/restores the vector along with the same id/width and cleanliness
status; concat reversal restores both operands and their prior vectors;
slice reversal restores the parent/view states. New-frame reversal keeps
its existing cleanliness/exclusivity hypothesis.
Binding a CC/R register to a region slot requires its ordered class
vector to equal that slot's selected, expanded §9.4 class vector, and,
with topology, its actual vertices to equal the inherited placement.
Check this with the existing width/disjointness preflight before any
effect or view consumption. Class vectors introduce no new alias or
storage-sharing permission, nor permission to relabel or move a wire.

A zero-width register binds no interface slot; its empty vector is used only by concat/slice/ledger rules.
At a CC/R call, failure of register-to-selected-frame class-vector or inherited-placement equality produces FAULT_UB_TRAP before any effect or view consumption.

`qreg[n]` is the type of a register whose width n is fixed statically;
`qreg[*]` the type of a register whose width is known only at run time
(an unsigned 64-bit n ≥ 0). A `qreg[n]` value may be used wherever a
`qreg[*]` is expected; a `qreg[*]` value bound to a `qreg[n]` parameter
or block parameter (`call`, `br`, `ret`, launch) or to a slot of width n
(region call, §4.7) is checked at that transfer: its width MUST equal
the required one. The `qreg.*` instructions execute in CC and R;
`measure` only in CC. An instruction whose precondition fails is a CC
trap / R fault (§4.9) with code `FAULT_UB_TRAP` unless the table says
otherwise, and has no effect. An id's value is chosen by the executor
(the suites compare ids only for equality); an id is never reassigned to
another register while the program is loaded. **Handle transitions:**

| Instruction | Precondition | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `q = qreg.new cls n` | `n` a literal n ≥ 1 (q: `qreg[n]`) or an `i64` value read unsigned, 0 included (q: `qreg[*]`); the device can provide n wires of class `cls`, else code `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP` | q live, clean, with a fresh id and wires disjoint from every live or viewed register. [deps: ISA-3.3-P7] |
| `qreg.free q` | q live, not a view, clean on every basis configuration (a verifying executor checks it; on failure q stays live and unchanged; a non-verifying one: §17 item 14) | q consumed |
| `q2 = qreg.slice q lo len` | q live, not a view; `lo` and `len` each a literal (`len` ≥ 1) or an `i64` value, both unsigned; lo + len ≤ n, computed without wrap (a trap even when literals make the failure evident) | q2 a view of wires [lo, lo+len) of q, of type `qreg[len]` for a literal len and `qreg[*]` otherwise; q viewed; q2 MUST be the operand of exactly one region call and of nothing else; after that call q2 is consumed and q is live again; a use of q while it is viewed traps (the static rule below covers the cases suite F rejects). Reversing that region call (§7.4) restores q viewed and q2 live; reversing the slice frame then restores q live |
| `q = qreg.concat q1 q2` | q1, q2 live, not views, distinct registers | q1, q2 consumed; q live, with a fresh id, over the wires of q1 then q2; `qreg[n1+n2]` if both operands are static, else `qreg[*]` |
| `w = qreg.width q` | q live, not a view | w: `i64`, the width; no state change |
| `id = qreg.id q` | q live, not a view | id: `i64`, the bits of q's id; no state change |
| `q = qreg.from_id id` | `id` is the id of a register of the program that is live, not a view, and not owned by another run that is not CLOSED (§10) | q: `qreg[*]`, an alias of that register; no ledger state change; the run now owns the register (§10), which reversal does not undo (ownership ends when the run is CLOSED) |
| region call (§4.7) with q as operand | q live (a view included); its width equals the slot's (§3.3 binding) and its ordered class vector equals the selected §9.4 slot vector (with topology, its actual vertices equal the inherited placement); the wire sets filling distinct wire/region slots are pairwise disjoint, and an explicit control is disjoint from targets and consumed/produced operands (read-only input overlap remains allowed), else CC trap / R fault FAULT_UB_TRAP before any effect, including any view consumption. [deps: ISA-3.3-P4, ISA-4.7, ISA-4.9] | q unchanged. [deps: ISA-4.7] |
| `r = measure q` / `r = measure.iN q` | q live, not a view; the form `measure.iN` (1 ≤ N ≤ 128) for a `qreg[*]`, whose width MUST be N (checked before measuring); for a `qreg[n]` either `measure` or `measure.iN` with N = n (checked statically) | q consumed, its wires released; r: `in` / `iN` |
| reversal of a `qreg.*` frame (§7.4, §10) | – | the ledger state before that frame (below). A `measure` frame is **never reversed**: a segment containing one cannot be uncalled (§7.4) and a run containing one is not reversible (§10) |

**Ledger frames and their reversal** (§7.4). A `qreg.new` frame records
the id, ordered class vector and width; its reversal releases that register without a
test: every later frame touching it has been undone first (LIFO) and the
run owns it exclusively (§10), so under T4's exactness hypothesis it is
clean, and otherwise its residual, within the declared bounds of the
undone realisations (§12.3), is discarded with it.
A `qreg.free` frame records the id, ordered class vector, width and whether its
cleanliness was verified; its reversal re-creates the register clean and
live with the same id, ordered class vector and width (every alias sees it live
again). A `qreg.slice` frame records the parent id, lo and len, a
`qreg.concat` frame the two operand ids and the new id; their reversal
restores the exact prior states, ordered class vectors and wire order without moving, copying
or measuring a wire. `qreg.width`, `qreg.id` and `qreg.from_id` change
no ledger state and their frames are pure (§7.4); if an `uncall` later
removes the register an alias refers to, every use of the alias traps.

**Binding**: a register of width n (`qreg[n]`, or `qreg[*]` of run-time
width n) fills a `wire[n]` slot bit for bit (bit 0 ↔ wire 0); it fills a
`region[A,W]` slot iff n = 2^A·W with cell i = bits [iW, (i+1)W); for a
`qreg[*]` the width is checked at the call, before any effect.
Host-supplied register ids are `qreg` values of the parameter's type.

The register ids supplied as arguments of one `bennett_run` MUST
denote pairwise disjoint registers, else `bennett_run` returns `E_ARG`
before executing the entry function or acquiring any register ownership.
[deps: ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-10]

**Registers, handles and aliases.** A `qreg` value is a reference to a
register of the device; the state live / viewed / consumed belongs to
the register, not to a name. `qreg` values pass unchanged as `call`
arguments, `ret` values and `br` block arguments (each transfer binds
another name to the same register and changes no state; a `qreg[*]`
reaching a `qreg[n]` parameter is width-checked, above). Two names bound
to one register (by such transfers or by `qreg.from_id`) are
**aliases**: a transition
made through either is observed through both. The wire operands of one
region call, with its effective control context, MUST denote pairwise
disjoint wires (§3.3 disjointness); aliases violate it. **Static and
run-time verdicts.** Suite F rejects exactly: two `qreg` operands of one
region call or of one `qreg.concat` that are the same name; and, on some path from a
`qreg.slice`, a use of the view's name other than as the operand of one
region call (a `br`, `call` or `ret` transfer included), its reaching a
`ret` or the end of the function unconsumed, or a use of the slice's
parent operand name before that call. Every other overlap, and every
use of a viewed register through another name, is a CC trap / R fault
with code `FAULT_UB_TRAP` before any effect.

### 3.4 Execution in CC and R
belongs to the target used for that VM closure and its reached native
leaves, not to the host machine instructions of the whole binary.
Certificate inventory and phase/control reconstruction cover that selected
closure's invocations. Each VM schedule pins the target/model hash even
when several modules share the target. Host qreg handles and angle values
are marshalled through the published launch bindings, not reinterpreted
as model basis indices. A native comparison uses the corresponding
materialized marked host entry and captured inputs; it does not require
every host instruction to have a quantum gate model.

The selected VM closure includes transitive callees of marked entries, whether or not those callees contain markers; native comparison materializes that same callee closure with the prescribed runtime and arithmetic libraries.

**Resource contexts and canonical displays (K87).**

For exactly one invocation context, resources and gates_used report its
§8.2 scheduled event accounting. resource_contexts may be omitted; if
present it MUST have exactly one matching record. The root context_key
is []. A standalone verified resource report binds one invocation and
an effective §11 schedule, including an explicit empty-entries schedule
when defaults supply all decisions. A schedule-free reference claim
retains K13's reference-only meaning and is not target-resource evidence.

For more than one invocation context, resource_contexts MUST contain
exactly one record for every §12.1 context, in increasing bytewise order
of the JCS encodings of their complete keys. Include default-covered and
predicated-off invocations. Full identity includes resolved source
point, loop indices, fixed-binding instance, forward semantic-call
occurrence and oracle step/phase where applicable, not just variant and
inherited-control count. Resolve each context's own args, token budgets,
placements, cleanup, epsilon shares, schedule/default decisions, native
leaves and wire intervals. Recompute every declared resource and gate
count from that context. gates_used counts native leaves after scheme
expansion, each once, with existing scheme/error attribution unchanged.

For compatibility, unkeyed resources/gates_used/estimate MUST be the
first canonical context's projection. An optional estimate's presence
and value agree with that projection. The projection discharges only
that context. Missing, duplicate, reordered, foreign or mismatched
context records, or a conflicting projection, reject the requested
report. A singleton array, when present, follows the same validation.

Whether or not estimate is present, accounting uses exact nonnegative
rational arithmetic. Each resources value is a canonical decimal string
using §10.1's 34-significant-digit upward encoding. For q>0, find integer
e with 10^e <= q < 10^(e+1), then display
ceil(q / 10^(e-33)) * 10^(e-33). Zero is "0". Spelling is ASCII digits with an optional decimal point; no exponent, no plus sign, no leading integer zeros except the value "0" itself, no trailing fractional zeros, no negative zero.

The checker compares the producer's canonical string with the canonical
encoding of its independently recomputed exact rational value, not the
numeric displayed decimal with that exact rational. Noncanonical strings
or differing encodings reject. Resource-row keys and class qualification
retain §8.2/§10.1's vector and escaping requirements; no scalar sum
replaces a required per-class row. Capacity, matching, objectives and
resource calculations continue to use exact rationals. Never add rounded
child displays to compute an exact parent value.

When estimate is present, precision_digits is 34 and its reduced
exact_resources fractions MUST also equal the recomputed values, with
nonnegative numerator and positive denominator decimal strings and zero
as 0/1. K12's existing mandatory-estimate cases and complete class rows
remain mandatory. Optional estimate here does not weaken K12. Omitting
estimate elsewhere does not mean a decimal must be an exact rational
representation; it is the defined upper display enclosure.

The root counts its realised event list once, including semantic callee
leaves. Child reports are independently checkable subtree/footprint views,
not extra costs added to the root. Measure child timing relative to that
invocation's entry, preserving internal delays in the effective schedule.
Restrict caller-owned interface and inherited-control lifetimes to that
invocation interval when displaying the child view. This neither
transfers ownership nor creates an additional charge to the root.
Within each view apply §8.2 dirty-borrow replacement: the borrower replaces
the owner's charge for that wire during the borrowed interval. There is
no second aggregation by summing child peak/time maps. Error-bound
multiplicities retain their existing separate composition rule.

For a non-K12 resource report, omission of estimate remains valid when displays match recomputed encodings; K12 estimate certificates still require estimate.

Closed-schema certificate readers require the K87 resource_contexts and canonical-display upgrade; legacy repeated-context flat reports require recomputation rather than an inferred max or sum.

**Resource-context witness (K87).** Let flip(t) have body x t, and let a root keep mutable t and in-role c live throughout:
Each root has its own [] context; a descriptor from another root is validated against the receiving root's bindings and placements. With a shared flip placed in A under root A and B under root B, copying the A descriptor into B's certificate rejects.
A homogeneous two-call realization accepts one uniform frame; a single-context frame_contexts array is also fully validated.

**Token equality bindings (K91).**

A resolved invocation carries a canonical token equality partition.
For an oracle invocation list its separate invocation token first, then
token-typed args in signature order. For an action, perm_action or CC/R
entry list only token-typed parameters in signature order: §4.7 action_call
has no separate invocation token. Budget fields do not create identities.
The separate token accepted by the estimation ABI supplies the budget,
but contributes an identity slot only for an oracle subject.
Equal labels denote one valid token identity and require equal tuples;
unequal labels denote distinct identities even when tuples agree.
Labels are assigned by first occurrence, starting at 0, without gaps.
An empty token list has []. A token-free oracle has [0]; a token-free
action/perm_action/CC/R entry has []. An action with a,b uses [0,0] for
one shared token identity and [0,1] for two distinct identities.
No process-local handle is serialized. params_hash remains SHA-256 of
the typed args array alone. Full invocation/schedule/cache matching
additionally compares token_aliases; params_hash alone is insufficient.

Executors derive this partition from actual live handles. Tuple-only
requests with token arguments must explicitly supply the partition;
malformed labels, wrong arity or tuple-inconsistent equality are E_ARG
before producing an estimate or performing an effect. Every consistent
partition is admitted. Source forwarding, lexical captures, lproc fixed
bindings and adjoint/inverse traversal preserve resolved identities.
A successful mint creates a fresh identity attached to that dynamic
defining occurrence; it is not reconstructed by tuple interning.
Nested contexts retain the identity mapping and canonicalize their own
ordered partition from it; equal local label numbers in two contexts
do not by themselves denote one global token.

This source-binding identity context is explicit metadata outside the
closed catalog JSON and its positional value arguments. K24's token
values and K77's exactly-one-field oracle_invocation token channel keep
their tuple encoding. Builders and captures additionally preserve the
identity context so source matching is not performed on tuple equality.

Claims used in an identity-dependent context bind its partition too.
Parameter-uniform structural proofs quantify over every consistent
partition as well as every argument vector/budget. Inverse-pair entries
bind the primary/argument token partition when relevant; coverage must
preserve it and all its tuple-equality constraints alongside the existing
primary-budget inequality and exact arg_tokens tests. A proof for one
partition cannot be reused for another merely because params_hash agrees.

A parameter-uniform structural claim uses invocation:{args:"all",budget:"all",token_aliases:"all"}; token_aliases:"all" quantifies over every partition consistent with each quantified argument vector and budget. Concrete invocations use canonical arrays.
Schedule/certificate-only changes preserve object_hash under unchanged header/source bytes; changing any header version requires recomputing object_hash and downstream bindings.
Closed-schema certificate and schedule readers require the K91 token_aliases upgrade.


In a mixed fat object, token_aliases binds invocations of the loaded
marked device ISA object and linked device closure. Execution derives
partitions from that program's valid live token handles and preserves
equality through device calls; tuple-based estimate/schedule requests
supply equality partitions explicitly. Serialization contains equality
classes, never host pointers or process-local handle ids. Alias-bearing
schedules and certificates bind that device object/root and invocation.
Native entry points, argv/envp and host-only libc need no device-alias
entries. The future mixed launch shim negotiates the alias-binding ABI
feature; §10 alone does not specify that shim or container interface.

**Action and inverse-pair witnesses (K91).**

The published token-free flip action's action_call has no token operand:
execution, estimates and replay all derive []. An action with token
parameters a,b has [0,0] for a=b or [0,1] for distinct equal-tuple tokens.
A CC sink wrapper has the same ordering. Only an oracle with primary T0
and token arguments a,b can use [0,1,1] versus [0,1,2].

### 10.1 Launch-estimate contract (K12)

**LAUNCH-ESTIMATE** (K12; b-isa-spec-c15, from bennett-vm-hsd).
The 0.9 request carries the complete resolved parameter vector.

**Token-alias ABI feature (K91).**

Both `bennett_estimate_run_with_token_aliases` and `bennett_sched_request_set_token_aliases` copy and validate the JCS array. Keep the old ABI callable; with no
token args infer [0] for an oracle and [] for an action/perm_action.
A legacy request with token arguments and no alias binding returns E_ARG;
the additive alias-aware function admits every consistent partition and
provides exact estimates of each resolved request. A bound over partitions
is not a K12 exact result. This request diagnostic does not make any valid
program or alias pattern illegal, and does not replace implementing the
full alias-aware capability. Publish
the extension under an explicit launch/sched ABI revision or feature
contract, including loader capability negotiation; do not change the
published C argument layout/symbol meaning silently.

The additive symbols and binding rules above define the launch/sched 0.9 feature `token_aliases/1`. Loader capability negotiation establishes support for both additive symbols and the token_aliases metadata contract before issuing an alias-aware request; symbol availability alone does not authorize silently dropping the partition. This feature retains the existing C argument layouts and symbol meanings.

**Resolved request.** `bennett_estimate_run` MUST copy and
validate `params_jcs`, the JCS array of **all** classical arguments in
signature order, encoded as §12.3 specifies; `params_len` is its UTF-8
byte length, excluding any NUL terminator. The separately supplied
valid token of that program fixes `[B,heap,stack,depth]`; token-typed
arguments fix their own tuples, never the outer tuple by default.
Host- and live device-minted tokens are both admitted (§5.4); the
request copies the tuple while valid, so later revocation cannot
retarget an already returned estimate.
An empty array binds an empty signature only; no argument is implicitly
zero, inferred from a hash, or taken from an earlier run. A missing,
extra, mistyped or out-of-range argument is `E_ARG` before producing an
estimate. Every well-typed resolved argument vector is supported; the
interface bundles, arbitrary target/fault entries, dirty wires and
interface cells remain quantum inputs, not chosen basis values.

The caller supplies a nonempty `variant` and `inherited_controls` as
in §11; these are part of the request, not inferred from a different
invocation. The request denotes that region/variant/control count at
its declared accuracy and those resolved parameters and budget.
It uses the object's schedule exactly when every §5.4/§11 binding
matches, otherwise the §11 defaults. The estimator MUST resolve the
complete generated realisation and aggregate its scheduled leaf events
and wire live intervals by §8.2; running one basis input, counting only
executed oracle rows instead of all padded B steps, or silently changing
parameters, budget, target, expansion library or cleanup is not an
estimate of that request. A consumer lacking the selected expansion
library reports `E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` as §11 requires (b-isa-spec-8r4);
this postpones mechanics, not the estimate capability.

**Resource rows.** `estimate_nresources` counts scalar rows,
not target resource declarations. A `count` or `time` resource has one
row named by its escaped resource name. Each `occupancy` or `peak`
resource has one row **for every target class**, including unused classes with value
zero, named `escape(resource)[escape(class)]`; no sum across classes is
substituted. `escape` percent-encodes the UTF-8 bytes except ASCII
letters, digits, `-._~`, using uppercase hex (including `%`, `[` and
`]`). Scalar resource names use the same escape. Rows are enumerated
by increasing bytewise encoded name. Thus names are injective even when
target names contain delimiters; `name` and `value_decimal` remain
ordinary NUL-terminated strings with §10's ownership lifetime.

**Decimal values.** Aggregation is exact rational arithmetic;
rounding occurs only at the accessor boundary. `value_decimal` is the
least nonnegative decimal with at most **34 significant digits** that
is ≥ the exact resource value. For q > 0 let e be the unique integer
with 10^e ≤ q < 10^(e+1); return
`ceil(q / 10^(e−33)) × 10^(e−33)`. Zero is `0`. The canonical spelling
uses ASCII digits and an optional decimal point, no exponent, plus
sign, leading integer zeros (except `0`), trailing fractional zeros or
negative zero. Exact integers and terminating rationals that fit are
unchanged. This is an upper enclosure, not a fabricated exact value for
1/3; capacity, matching, objective comparison and certificate checking
MUST use the exact rationals, never the rounded display.

**Certificate binding.** The estimate's certificate MUST bind
object, interface, closure, target and effective schedule hashes, root
variant/controls, accuracy, expansion identity and the resolved
`bindings.invocation` (§12.3). Even a default-only realisation has an
explicit empty-entries schedule document with those bindings, hence a
schedule hash. Its root region has `estimate: { precision_digits: 34,
exact_resources: { "<encoded row name>": { numerator: "<integer>",
denominator: "<positive integer>" } } }`; fractions are reduced,
nonnegative and decimal-string encoded, zero is 0/1. `resources` uses
the identical encoded keys and canonical decimal strings returned by
`estimate_get`. A checker MUST rebuild the bound realisation, recompute
all exact rows and their upward decimal encodings, and reject any
binding, key, rational or display mismatch. A producer's resource map
alone is not verification.

**LAUNCH-ESTIMATE witness**

```json
{
  "variant":"forward","inherited_controls":0,
  "binding": {"args":["2"],"budget":["2","0","0","0"]},
  "precision_digits": 34,
  "classes": ["A","B]","idle"],
  "live": [
    {"class":"A","count":2,"start":"0","end":"1/3"},
    {"class":"B]","count":1,"start":"0","end":"1/3"}
  ],
  "rows": [
    ["peak_qubits[A]","2"],
    ["peak_qubits[B%5D]","1"],
    ["peak_qubits[idle]","0"],
    ["qubit_second[A]","0.6666666666666666666666666666666667"],
    ["qubit_second[B%5D]","0.3333333333333333333333333333333334"],
    ["qubit_second[idle]","0"],
    ["time","0.3333333333333333333333333333333334"]
  ],
  "decimal_checks": [
    {"rational":"0","decimal":"0"},
    {"rational":"2","decimal":"2"},
    {"rational":"1/8","decimal":"0.125"},
    {"rational":"1/3","decimal":"0.3333333333333333333333333333333334"},
    {"rational":"2/3","decimal":"0.6666666666666666666666666666666667"},
    {"rational":"1/30","decimal":"0.03333333333333333333333333333333334"},
    {"rational":"10/3","decimal":"3.333333333333333333333333333333334"}
  ]
}
```

This **aggregation witness** assumes the displayed scheduled intervals,
unit class rates and no other events; it does not assert an unbound
oracle gate count. A permitted cost expression `1 / 3` produces that
makespan. The exact class peaks are 2 and 1, not a replacement scalar 3;
occupancies are 2/3 and 1/3, and the idle class is present. Changing the
argument `2` to `1` denotes a different request, even at the same budget.
No 0.8 request can transmit that distinction through §10's signature.

## 11. Scheduling interface (sched 0.9)
### 4.7 Region calls (structured code, CC and R)

`oracle_call @o tk (ins..) tgt fr (mem..) (args..)`; `action_call @a
(mut..) (ins..) (mem..) (args..)`; `adj action_call …`; `adj oracle_call
…` ≡ `oracle_call …`; `ctl w <call>` ≡ the call under `qpred w`. In CC
and R, wire and region slots are filled by `qreg` operands per §3.3
(§3.1: R functions call Q regions exactly as CC does; the call is one
frame of the R history, §7.4). In an action, a region operand names an
interface region of the caller or a local `qregion` of the caller
(§4.6); a local region fills an `in` or a `mut` slot (an `in` slot
returns it unchanged; the caller restores it to its image before exit
either way, §5.1). The `mem` operands of lifted rows follow §4.2.
**Classical arguments** have the signature's types `iN`, `ptr` (a value
of width ptr_width), `f64` and `token`. In an oracle body or lproc an
`iN` or `ptr` argument is a pseudo-location of width N or ptr_width, an
`f64` argument a pseudo-location of width 64 holding its binary64 bits,
and a `token` argument is usable only as the `tk` operand of
`lift.oracle_call`/`lift.oracle_uncall`, in a region call's `args` list,
or in `lift.call`'s token list. Lproc classical parameters have these
same bit readings and may also be forwarded in `lift.call`'s classical
list; token parameters retain their token identity and validity (§5.4).
The invocation operand `tk` is separate from the callee's declared
`args`; its explicit catalog context channel is `oracle_invocation`
(§11.1, K77), including for the lifted calls of §4.2.

      "fix": "In §10, immediately after the adapter-descriptor paragraph, add: 'The descriptor MUST enumerate the selected device closure's boundary-shared global dependencies, including dependencies of entries with no pointer or aggregate arguments. Each binding associates a device program/global slot with the resolved native storage identity, extent and registration generation. All selected entries referring to the same native definition MUST bind the same canonical allocation; identical names or initial bytes alone do not establish identity. The native storage is authoritative: loading or unloading a device program MUST NOT reinitialize it. Before each CC/R invocation, current native bytes and required metadata are acquired under the lease, either through a live mapping or alias-preserving copy-in. Closing publishes the exact remaining prefix, and subsequent invocations observe intervening native edits. A device layout image is not a replacement for this binding.'
